刚才	gāng cái	just now; a moment ago — 刚 gāng (just; barely) + 才 cái. A time word for the very recent past, the moment that has only just passed: 刚才他还在这儿 "he was still here just now." Here 才 keeps its sense of a threshold only just crossed.
钢笔	gāng bǐ	fountain pen — 钢 gāng (steel) + 笔 bǐ. The fountain pen, named for its steel nib. Its cousins fill out the modern desk: 圆珠笔 yuánzhūbǐ (ballpoint), 毛笔 máobǐ (brush), 铅笔 qiānbǐ (pencil).
高考	Gāo kǎo	college entrance exam
高楼	gāo lóu	tall building, tower
高硼硅玻璃	gāo péng guī bō li	borosilicate glass — "High boron-silicon glass," resistant to thermal shock. 耐热 (nàirè) is heat-resistant, 温差 (wēnchā) the temperature difference it can survive, 爆裂 (bàoliè) bursting or shattering.
高山	gāo shān	high mountain; alpine — 高 (high) + 山 (mountain). A tall mountain , the compound appears in geography, poetry, and cultural discourse.
高手	gāo shǒu	expert; master; highly skilled person — 高 (high, superior) + 手 (hand; skill; person with a skill). A superior hand , someone with excellent technique. 象棋高手 (chess master); 编程高手 (programming expert); 武林高手 (martial arts master, from wuxia fiction). Colloquial and approving. The opposite is 新手 xīnshǒu (newcomer, novice).
高铁	gāo tiě	high-speed rail — Trains are booked on the official 12306 app or site (it has an English version) or through travel apps, and your passport is the ticket: it is checked at the station entrance and at the gate. 动车 (dòngchē) are slightly slower bullet trains. Arrive early; large stations have security checks.
高兴得跳起来	gāo xìng de tiào qǐ lai	so happy (she) jumped up — adj + 得 + result — 得 can also follow an adjective (not just a verb) when the adjective itself takes a result clause. The structure is Adj + 得 + [result] , "so [adj] that [result]." This is the correlative "so…that" pattern.
高原	gāo yuán	plateau; highlands
搞笑	gǎo xiào	funny; hilarious
哥儿俩	gēr liǎ	the two brothers; the pair of bros
哥哥	gē ge	elder brother — The base word: 哥 doubled, the second syllable toned down to neutral. Standard everyday term for one's own older brother, and a child can also use it for an unrelated older boy.
哥们儿	gē men r	buddy, bro, mate — 哥 + 们 (plural) + the Beijing 儿 ending. Close male friends, sworn-brother style. Singular or plural, it marks loyalty between guys; northern and casual, common in Beijing speech.
歌唱	gē chàng	to sing (in praise); singing as an art — 歌 gē (song) + 唱 chàng (to sing). More formal and elevated than 唱歌. Used for singing as a discipline and for singing in celebration: 歌唱家 gēchàngjiā is a professional vocalist, and 歌唱祖国 means to sing the praises of one's homeland.
歌词	gē cí	song lyrics; the words of a song — 歌 gē (song) + 词 cí (wording). The words set to music, as opposed to 曲 qǔ, the melody. A songwriter is often credited separately for 作词 zuòcí (writing the lyrics) and 作曲 zuòqǔ (composing the tune).
歌剧	gē jù	opera — 歌 (song) plus 剧 (drama). A sung drama; the term used for Western-style opera, as distinct from 京剧 (jīngjù) Peking opera.
歌曲	gē qǔ	song (as a work) — 歌 (song) plus 曲 (tune, melody). 歌 leans on the lyrics, 曲 on the music; together they name a song as a finished piece. Slightly more formal than a bare 歌.
歌手	gē shǒu	singer — a skilled vocalist — 歌 gē (song) + 手 shǒu (skilled person). The standard word for a professional singer. This -手 suffix pattern for skilled persons: 歌手 singer, 选手 athlete/contestant, 鼓手 drummer, 射手 archer/shooter, 旗手 flag-bearer.
歌星	gē xīng	singing star
格物	gé wù	investigation of things; rectification of affairs — A term from the Great Learning 大学, interpreted radically differently by Zhu Xi and Wang Yangming. For Zhu Xi: the patient investigation of external things to grasp their 理.
个别	gè bié	individual; a small number of; exceptional — 个 gè (single) + 别 bié (separate; other). Either "one by one, individually" (个别谈话 "to talk to people one at a time") or "only a few, exceptional" (个别情况 "a few isolated cases").
个人	gèr én	individual; personal; in one's own view — 个 gè (single) + 人 rén (person). The single person set against the group, and by extension "personal, private": 个人观点 "a personal opinion," 个人信息 "personal information." Its foil is 集体 jítǐ (the collective).
个体	gè tǐ	an individual (entity); a single member — 个 gè (single) + 体 tǐ (body; entity). A single unit within a larger whole, used in biology and economics alike: 个体差异 "individual differences," 个体户 "a self-employed business owner."
个子	gè zi	height; stature; build (of a person) — 个 gè + 子 zi (noun-suffix). A person's height or physical build: 高个子 "tall build," 矮个子 "short build," 个子很高 "very tall." Used of the whole frame of a person, not a measured number.
各自	gè zì	each (on their own); respectively — 各 gè (each) + 自 zì (self). Each person doing a thing for their own self, separately: 各自回家 "each went home on their own." A near relative of 己, built on the same idea of the individual self set apart from the group.
给面子	gěi miàn zi	to give face; to show respect; to honor someone's standing — 给 (to give) + 面子. To actively enhance or protect someone else's face. Can mean: attending their event (your presence honors them), publicly praising them, deferring to them in front of others, or not challenging them when they are wrong , to spare their embarrassment.
根本	gēn běn	fundamental; basic; (with a negative) at all — 根 gēn (root) + 本 běn (root). The two words for "root" doubled. As an adjective, fundamental or essential: 根本问题 "the fundamental issue." As an adverb with a negative, it means "not at all": 我根本不知道 "I had no idea whatsoever."
更多	gèng duō	even more (quantity) — 更 gèng (even more) + 多 duō (many; much). The most frequent collocation for comparative quantity. Appears in both spoken and written registers without restriction.
更改	gēng gǎi	to alter; to change (formal) — 更 gēng (to change) + 改 gǎi (to alter; to revise). A formal compound for changing plans, decisions, or documents. More official in tone than 改变 gǎibiàn and preferred in administrative contexts.
更好	gèng hǎo	even better — 更 gèng + 好 hǎo (good). The quintessential comparison pair. Appears in everyday speech, advertising, and formal writing with equal frequency.
更何况	gèng hé kuàng	let alone; much less; to say nothing of — 更 gèng + 何况 hékuàng (how much more; not to mention). A rhetorical connective that escalates from a difficult or surprising premise to an even more extreme conclusion. Introduces the harder case: if X is already the case, 更何况 Y is the clincher.
更换	gēng huàn	to replace; to switch out — 更 gēng (to change) + 换 huàn (to swap; to exchange). To replace one thing with another of the same kind: a part, a component, a person in a role. Emphasizes substitution more than 更改, which emphasizes revision.
更加	gèng jiā	even more; still more (emphatic) — 更 gèng + 加 jiā (to add; to increase). A compound adverb that amplifies the degree of 更 further. Preferred in formal writing, speeches, and academic prose. In speech, bare 更 is more natural; 更加 in conversation can sound slightly elevated.
更新	gēng xīn	to update; to renew; to replace with something new — 更 gēng (to change; to replace) + 新 (new). To make new again: to update software (更新系统), to renew a contract (更新合同), to replace aging infrastructure (更新设备). A standard verb in technology, administration, and policy. 持续更新 (continuous updating) is a phrase familiar to every software user.
更衣	gēng yī	to change clothes; dressing room — 更 gēng (to change) + 衣 yī (clothes; garment). To change one's clothing. Formal and classical in register; in modern spoken Chinese 换衣服 huàn yīfu is more natural. Survives today primarily in 更衣室 gēngyīshì (changing room; locker room).
工厂	gōng chǎng	factory
工程	gōng chéng	engineering
工程师	gōng chéng shī	engineer — 工程 gōngchéng (engineering; project , lit. "work + process") + 师 shī (master; teacher; professional). Engineer , one of the most respected professional titles in China, associated with the nation's modernization drive. 工程 alone means a large project or undertaking: 三峡工程 = Three Gorges Project.
工具	gōng jù	tool; instrument
工人	gōng rén	worker; laborer
工业	gōng yè	industry; the industrial sector — 工 gōng (labor; work) + 业 yè (sector). Manufacturing industry as a branch of the economy: 重工业 "heavy industry," 工业化 "industrialization." Paired with 农业 (agriculture) in the standard rhetoric of national development.
工业区	gōng yè qū	industrial zone
工艺	gōng yì	craft; technique; craftsmanship — 工 gōng (work; skill) + 艺 yì (art; skill; technique). Craft as the intersection of technical skill and artistry. 工艺品 gōngyìpǐn = handicraft item; artisan product. 传统工艺 = traditional crafts. 工艺美术 = arts and crafts (as a discipline).
工资	gōng zī	salary; wages
公案	gōng àn	kōan — a paradoxical case used as a meditation object — 公 gōng (public) + 案 àn (case/record). Originally a legal term: a judicial precedent. In Chan, a 公案 is a recorded exchange between a master and student, or a question posed by the master, used as a meditation object.
公差	gōng chā	tolerance — The allowed deviation from a dimension. Tight tolerances cost money; for parts that thread or seal against each other they decide whether the product works. 超差 (chāochā) is out of tolerance.
公共	gōng gòng	public; communal; shared by all — 公 gōng (public) + 共 gòng (common; together). Belonging to or used by the community at large: 公共汽车 "public bus," 公共场所 "public place." The plain modifier for anything held in common.
公鸡	gōng jī	rooster, cock — 公 (male) + 鸡 (chicken). The crowing bird of the farmyard, the one Shuōwén praised for knowing the hour.
公斤	gōng jīn	kilogram — 公 gōng (metric) + 斤 jīn (a traditional unit of weight, about half a kilo). The standard kilogram. The 公 again marks the international unit against the older 斤.
公开	gōng kāi	open; public; to make public — 公 gōng (public) + 开 kāi (to open). Out in the open rather than hidden: as an adjective, 公开信 "an open letter"; as a verb, 公开秘密 "to make a secret public." The opposite of 秘密 mìmì (secret).
公筷	gōng kuài	Serving chopsticks , a second pair of chopsticks placed at the table for taking from shared dishes, kept separate from the pair used for eating. The practice is hygienic and has become more widespread post-2020. At formal banquets, a serving spoon (公勺 gōng sháo) may accompany each dish.
公里	gōng lǐ	kilometer — 公 (gōng, public, metric/standardized) + 里 (Chinese li, 500 m). The standard modern unit of distance in China: 公里 = 1 kilometer = 2 市里 (shìlǐ, "market li," the standard li of 500 m).
公路	gōng lù	highway, public road
公牛	gōng niú	bull
公平	gōng píng	fair; just; impartial — 公 gōng (public; impartial) + 平 píng (level). Even-handed dealing that favors no side: 公平竞争 "fair competition," 这不公平 "that's not fair." The everyday word for fairness.
公式	gōng shì	Formula — Formula: "public pattern," a form that holds generally. 套公式 (tào gōngshì, "slot into the formula") is the study-hall phrase for plug-and-chug problem solving.
公司	gōng sī	company; firm; corporation — 公 gōng (public; common) + 司 sī (to manage; an office). A jointly run enterprise, a body managed in common: 一家公司 "a company." Note the contrast with the homophone 私 sī (private).
公园	gōng yuán	park; public garden — 公 gōng (public) + 园 yuán (garden). A garden held in common, open to all, as opposed to a private 花园 huāyuán. One of the first 公 words a beginner meets.
公章	gōng zhāng	company seal — A Chinese company binds itself with its red seal more than with a signature. 合同专用章 (hétong zhuānyòngzhāng) is a seal used only for contracts and is equally valid.
公正	gōng zhèng	just; impartial; fair-minded — 公 gōng (impartial) + 正 zhèng (upright). The public standard joined to the straight line: justice as judgment held to the same mark for all. Common of judges, verdicts, and arbiters: 公正的判决 "a fair verdict."
公众	gōng zhòng	the public; general public
攻读	gōng dú	to pursue (a degree, a field of study); to read-for — 攻 gōng (to attack; to apply oneself to) + 读. The verb for actively pursuing advanced study , a master's, a doctorate, a specialty. The 攻 conveys the sense of going at it, attacking the subject rather than passively absorbing.
供应	gōng yìng	to supply; to provide; supply — 供 gōng (to provide) + 应 yìng (to meet a need). To supply goods or services to meet a demand: 供应商 (supplier), 供应链 (supply chain), 供不应求 (supply falls short of demand). The yìng here is the answering of a need with provision.
供应链	gōng yìng liàn	supply chain
供应商	gōng yìng shāng	supplier
宫保鸡丁	gōng bǎo jī dīng	Kung Pao Chicken
恭喜	gōng xǐ	to congratulate; congratulations — 恭 gōng (respectful) + 喜 xǐ (joy). To respectfully share in someone's happiness. Doubled as 恭喜恭喜 it is the warm spoken "congratulations," and 恭喜发财 gōngxǐ fācái is the standard New Year greeting wishing wealth.
恭喜发财	gōng xǐ fā cái	The classic New Year greeting — The classic New Year greeting: "congratulations and may you get rich." Especially used in Southern China and the diaspora. The Cantonese pronunciation (gung1 hei2 faat3 choi4) is equally well-known internationally.
共和	gòng hé	republic; shared harmony
共同	gòng tóng	common; shared; jointly; together — 共 gòng (together; shared) + 同 tóng (same). Held in common by all parties: 共同的目标 "a shared goal," 共同努力 "to strive together." Stronger and more formal than 一起.
沟通	gōu tōng	to communicate; to bridge; to reach an understanding — 沟 gōu (a ditch; a channel; a conduit) + 通 (to connect; to make pass through). Originally to link two waterways by a channel; now overwhelmingly used for communication between people: to open a channel of understanding so meaning can flow back and forth.
狗叫	gǒu jiào	a dog barks — 狗 + 叫 (jiào, to call out, cry). The plain way to say a dog is barking. 汪汪 (wāngwāng) is the onomatopoeia for the sound itself.
狗粮	gǒu liáng	dog food
狗窝	gǒu wō	kennel, doghouse
狗熊	gǒu xióng	black bear; coward — 狗 + 熊 (xióng, bear). Literally a dog-bear, the colloquial name for the Asian black bear. Figuratively 狗熊 brands a coward, the opposite of a 英雄 (yīngxióng, hero), a pun that turns on the rhyme.
骨力	gǔ lì	bone-force; structural strength in a stroke
固定	gù dìng	fixed; to fix in place; regular — 固 gù (solid; firm) + 定 dìng (fixed). Held firmly in one place or pattern, not movable or variable: 固定收入 "a fixed income," 把架子固定在墙上 "to fix the shelf to the wall."
故宫	Gù gōng	the Forbidden City; the Old Palace — 故 (old, former) + 宫 (gōng, palace). Literally "the Old Palace," the formal Chinese name for the former imperial palace complex in Beijing, known in English as the Forbidden City. It was the residence of emperors of the Ming and Qing dynasties and is now the Palace Museum (故宫博物院, Gùgōng Bówùyuàn).
故人	gù rén	old friend; an acquaintance from former days — 故 (old, former) + 人 (rén, person). An old friend, specifically one known from an earlier period of life, now perhaps far away or long unseen. 故人 belongs to the literary and poetic register, common in classical verse about parting and reunion; the everyday equivalent is 老朋友 (lǎo péngyou).
故乡	gù xiāng	hometown; native place; homeland — 故 (old, former) + 乡 (xiāng, countryside, native village). The place one is from, charged with the weight of belonging and longing. 故乡 is the literary and emotional register; the plainer 家乡 (jiāxiāng) is more neutral and everyday.
故意	gù yì	deliberately; on purpose; intentionally — 故 (deliberate) + 意 (yì, intention, will, mind). To do something on purpose, with conscious intent. Used adverbially: 他故意迟到 (he was late on purpose), 不是故意的 (didn't mean to, it wasn't intentional).
故障	gù zhàng	malfunction; breakdown; fault — 故 (cause; here, an underlying fault) + 障 (zhàng, obstruction, barrier). A malfunction in a machine or system: something with a cause behind it that obstructs normal operation.
顾面子	gù miàn zi	to consider face; to save face for someone; to protect dignity — 顾 gù (to consider; to look after; to take into account). 顾面子 means to take face into consideration when making decisions or acting , whether one's own face or someone else's. More deliberate and conscious than simply 给面子. Suggests a thoughtful navigation of face dynamics.
顾问	gù wèn	adviser; consultant — 顾 gù (to look after; to consider) + 问 wèn (to ask). The person one turns to and asks for counsel: 法律顾问 "legal adviser," 技术顾问 "technical consultant." Literally the one whom you regard and question.
怪不得	guài bu de	"That explains it!" Synonym for 难怪 with the same function — "That explains it!" Synonym for 难怪 with the same function. Slightly more drawn out and emphatic. 怪不得他不来 ("that's why he didn't come").
关闭	guān bì	to close (formal)
关灯	guān dēng	to turn off the light
关帝	Guān Dì	Emperor Guan — deified general, god of war and righteousness — Guan Yu 关羽 (d. 220 CE), the historical Three Kingdoms general celebrated for his loyalty and righteousness 义 yì, was progressively deified over the Tang, Song, and Ming dynasties, eventually receiving the title 关圣帝君 (Holy Emperor Guan).
关汉卿	Guān Hàn qīng	greatest Yuan playwright
关怀	guān huái	to show concern and care for (warm, often institutional)
关机	guān jī	to power off (a device); to switch off a phone
关键	guān jiàn	key, crucial, the decisive hinge
关键在于	guān jiàn zài yú	"The key is" or "what matters is — "The key is" or "what matters is." Marks the core of an argument. Useful when you want to highlight the deciding factor among several you have just laid out: 关键在于时间 ("the key is timing").
关节	guān jié	joint (of the body); juncture — 关 guān (to join; pass) + 节 jié (joint). An anatomical joint, the hinge where bones meet: 膝关节 "knee joint." By extension, a critical juncture in a matter, the place where things turn.
关联	guān lián	correlation; connection; to be associated with — 关 (connection) + 联 (to join; to link). More formal and analytical than 关系 , used for logical correlations, data relationships, and systematic connections. Common in academic, research, and analytical writing. 相互关联 xiānghù guānlián = mutually correlated; interconnected.
关门	guān mén	to close the door; (of a shop) to close for the day or for good
关起来	guān qǐ lái	to lock up; to shut in; to imprison
关系到	guān xi dào	to affect; to have a bearing on; to be relevant to — 关系到 (lit. "the relationship reaches to") means that something has a bearing on, affects, or is relevant to something else. Used for high-stakes matters where the connection is important to note explicitly. Common in official, journalistic, and persuasive writing.
关系网	guān xi wǎng	network of connections; relationship network — 网 wǎng = net; network. 关系网 is the spider's web of personal connections , who you know, who they know, and the overlapping obligations and trust that run through the web.
关系重大	guān xi zhòng dà	of great importance; high-stakes; consequential — A common fixed expression used to characterize something as having major consequences or importance. 重大 zhòngdà = major, significant. Used in formal registers to signal that a decision, situation, or event carries real weight and should be treated with corresponding seriousness.
关心	guān xīn	to care about; concern; care — 关 (to concern; to be connected to) + 心 (heart). The heart connected to something , caring. One of the most important relational verbs in Chinese. Both the action of caring and the expression of concern. High frequency in family, friendship, and professional contexts.
关于	guān yú	about; concerning; with respect to — 关 guān (to connect) + 于 yú (to). Marks the subject matter of a book, talk, or document: 关于历史的书 "a book about history." Where 对于 frames an attitude, 关于 frames a topic.
关羽	guān yǔ	Guan Yu — the archetype of loyalty and honour — The red-faced sworn brother of Liu Bei, towering in loyalty and martial valour, who refuses every bribe and honour Cao Cao offers to keep him.
关注	guān zhù	to pay attention to; to follow closely; attention; concern — 关 (to concern; to connect) + 注 (to pour; to focus , water pouring into a single point). Focused, sustained attention toward something important. In digital contexts, 关注 means to follow someone's account (social media).
观沧海	Guān Cāng hǎi	"Gazing at the Vast Sea" — an early landscape poem — Written after Cao Cao's northern campaign of 207 CE, often cited as the first fully realized landscape poem in the tradition. The poet gazes from a cliff and the sea, swallowing sun and moon, becomes the mirror of his own ambition.
观察	guān chá	to observe; to watch closely; observation — 观 guān (to look) + 察 chá (to examine). To watch with attention in order to learn something: a scientist, a doctor, or a detective 观察 their subject. Stronger and more deliberate than simply watching.
观点	guān diǎn	viewpoint; point of view; standpoint — 观 guān (view) + 点 diǎn (point). The particular angle from which you regard an issue: 我同意你的观点 "I agree with your view." The opinion you argue from, lighter and more debatable than a fixed 观念.
观看	guān kàn	to watch; to view (an event or show) — 观 guān (to view) + 看 kàn (to look). To watch a performance, game, or spectacle. A slightly formal partner to the everyday 看 kàn, fitting for events that draw an audience.
观想	guān xiǎng	visualization — contemplating the Pure Land and Amitābha in detail — 观 guān (contemplate; observe) + 想 xiǎng (imagine; think). The more advanced and demanding practice outlined in the 观无量寿经 (Contemplation Sutra): systematic visualization of the Western Pure Land (its jeweled trees, lotus pools, the thrones, the light) and ultimately of Amitābha himself in full detail…
观音	Guān yīn	the bodhisattva 观自在 by another name
观众	guān zhòng	audience; spectators; viewers — 观 guān (to watch) + 众 zhòng (the many, a crowd). The watching crowd: the audience at a show, the spectators at a game, the viewers of a broadcast. Its cousins are 听众 tīngzhòng (listeners) and 读者 dúzhě (readers).
官方	guān fāng	official; governmental
管理	guǎn lǐ	to manage; to administer; management — 管 guǎn (to govern; to be in charge of) + 理 lǐ (to put in order). To run and oversee an organization, system, or resource: 管理公司 "to manage a company," 时间管理 "time management."
贯穿	guàn chuān	to run through, to permeate
光杆动词	guāng gǎn dòng cí	the bare verb: habits, facts, general truths — The unmarked verb is the default for habitual actions and standing facts, usually anchored by a frequency word: 我每天喝咖啡 (I drink coffee every day), 他住在北京 (he lives in Beijing), 地球绕太阳转 (the earth orbits the sun). No particle needed; adding 了 here would wrongly signal a completed event.
光明	guāng míng	light; radiance; a bright future — 光 guāng (light; beam) + 明 míng (bright). More elevated and abstract than 明亮. Used in spiritual and political registers: 光明大道 "the road of light" (a righteous path), 走向光明 "moving toward the light." The opposite is 黑暗 hēi'àn darkness.
逛街	guàng jiē	to stroll around shopping — 逛 (to stroll, wander) + 街 (street). A verb-object pair: to roam the streets with no fixed errand, window-shopping as much as buying. The default leisure activity in any Chinese city.
规定	guī dìng	to stipulate; a rule; a regulation — 规 guī (rule; compass) + 定 dìng (to fix). To lay down a rule in advance, and the rule so laid down. 按照规定 ànzhào guīdìng "according to the regulations." The formal, institutional cousin of 决定.
贵公司	guì gōng sī	your company (honorific) — The polite way to refer to the other side's company; 贵厂 (guì chǎng) for a factory. Your own side is plain 我们公司 or 我们. 贵 is the same character as "expensive," here meaning "honoured."
滚蛋	gǔn dàn	get lost, scram — 滚 (gǔn, to roll) + 蛋: a blunt order to get out. The image is of telling someone to roll away like a dropped egg. It is rude and forceful, used only in anger.
国歌	guó gē	national anthem — 国 (nation) plus 歌 (song). The official song of a country, sung standing at ceremonies and medal podiums.
国画	guó huà	traditional Chinese painting — 国 guó (nation) + 画 huà (painting). The native tradition of brush-and-ink painting on silk or paper, kin to calligraphy. Short for 中国画 Zhōngguóhuà, and set against 油画 yóuhuà, the imported oil-painting tradition.
国际	guó jì	international — 国 guó + 际 jì (boundary; border; between). "Between countries." The standard word for anything international: 国际机场 international airport · 国际关系 international relations · 国际化 internationalization.
国情	guó qíng	national conditions; the state of the nation — 国 (country) + 情 (conditions, circumstances, feelings). The specific circumstances of a given country , its history, population, resources, culture, and current state.
国外	guó wài	abroad
国语	Guó yǔ	Taiwan term for Mandarin
果肉	guǒ ròu	fruit pulp, flesh of fruit — 果 (guǒ, fruit) + 肉 (flesh). The soft edible part between the skin and the pit, the meat of the fruit.
果实	guǒ shí	fruit; the fruits (of labor) — 果 guǒ (fruit) + 实 shí (the ripened, full thing). The fruit a plant bears, and by extension the rewards of effort: 劳动的果实 "the fruits of labor." This is the seed-and-fruit sense of 实 made explicit.
过错	guò cuò	fault, wrongdoing
过分	guò fèn	excessive; going too far
过关	guò guān	to pass the pass; to clear a hurdle, exam, or test
过河	guò hé	to cross a river — 过 (guò, to cross) + 河 (river). A verb-object pair, to get to the far bank. It anchors several proverbs, since crossing water is the classic image of a one-way commitment.
过来	guò lái	come over, come here
过年	guò nián	to celebrate New Year
过去	guò qù	the past; to pass; to go over to — 过 guò (to pass; to cross; to go beyond) + 去 qù (away). As a noun: the past , what has passed away from the present. As a verb: to go over to (move across and away to a location). Both meanings share the same root image: time or a person crossing and moving beyond the current point.
过一会儿	guò yí huì r	after a while; in a little while — "After a spell passes," used to push something a short way into the future: 过一会儿再打给你 ("I'll call you back in a little while"). 过 (to pass) marks the elapsing of the short time. A close, even softer relative is 等会儿 / 待会儿 ("in a bit, later"), the ready phrase for "hold on / later."
还不如	hái bù rú	would still be better to (spoken)
还差得远	hái chà de yuǎn	"still far from good enough" — modest self-assessment; "I have a long way to go" — 还 hái (still; yet) + 差 chà (to fall short; to be lacking) + 得 de (degree complement marker) + 远 yuǎn (far). A self-deprecating phrase that positions the speaker as still well short of a standard , pointing forward to future growth rather than backward at achievement.
还好	hái hǎo	not bad; passable; fortunately (context-dependent) — The most versatile of the three hedges. As a standalone response to "how was it?" or "how are you?", 还好 reads as "fine, okay, not bad." As an opener to a clause, it shifts to "fortunately, luckily" , the speaker is noting that things didn't go as badly as they might have.
还是	hái shi	or (in a choice question) — Used in questions that offer the listener a choice between two (or more) options: A 还是 B？It signals "which one?" and asks for a pick. A choice question with 还是 does not also take 吗, since 还是 already makes it a question. The options can be nouns, verbs, or whole clauses.
还是算了	hái shì suàn le	forget it; never mind; on second thought, no
还有一点	hái yǒu yī diǎn	"One more thing" or "and another point — "One more thing" or "and another point." Marks the addition of a further item to a list of points you are making. Common in explanations and arguments where you are building up to a conclusion.
孩童	hái tóng	child; young children (literary) — 孩 paired with 童 (child, youngster). A bookish, slightly elevated word for children, common in writing and formal speech rather than casual talk.
孩子	hái zi	child; kid; one's offspring — 孩 (child) plus the neutral-tone suffix 子, which turns many one-syllable nouns into stable everyday words. 孩子 is the default word for a child and also the word a parent uses for a son or daughter of any age.
孩子叫雨淋湿了	hái zi jiào yǔ lín shī le	the child got soaked by the rain (colloquial 叫 passive) — 叫 jiào as passive marker: Subject + 叫 + Agent + Verb + Result. The most distinctly northern/colloquial of the three. Rain soaking a child is the kind of minor adversarial act that all four passive markers handle naturally.
海报	hǎi bào	poster — 海 (hǎi, sea) plus 报: a public announcement spread far and wide. The word for a poster or playbill, the kind pasted on a wall to advertise a film or a show.
海边	hǎi biān	seaside; the coast; the shore — 海 hǎi (sea) + 边 biān (edge; side). The edge where land meets sea, the place you go to be beside the water: 去海边度假 "to vacation at the seaside."
海岛	hǎi dǎo	island (in the sea) — 海 hǎi (sea) + 岛 dǎo (island). A piece of land surrounded by sea. Where 岛 alone is any island, 海岛 stresses the open-sea setting, far from the mainland.
海关	hǎi guān	customs (the office or checkpoint) — 海 hǎi (sea) + 关 guān (pass; barrier). The customs authority that inspects goods crossing a border, named for the sea-passage through which trade once arrived: 过海关 "to clear customs."
海军	hǎi jūn	navy — 海 hǎi (sea) + 军 jūn (army; armed force). The naval branch of the military, the "sea army." Its siblings are 陆军 lùjūn (the army, "land force") and 空军 kōngjūn (the air force).
海派	hǎi pài	Shanghai style — Shanghai style: the cosmopolitan, eclectic aesthetic and cultural orientation associated with Shanghai. Applied to art, architecture, cuisine, fashion, and urban manners. A term of both pride and occasional condescension from those outside Shanghai.
海上	hǎi shàng	maritime
海外	hǎi wài	overseas; abroad
海峡	hǎi xiá	strait (Taiwan Strait)
海鲜	hǎi xiān	seafood; fresh from the sea
海洋	hǎi yáng	ocean; the seas (collectively) — 海 hǎi (sea) + 洋 yáng (the open ocean). The full body of the world's salt water, the scientific and grand register: 海洋生物 "marine life," 太平洋是最大的海洋 "the Pacific is the largest ocean."
含有	hán yǒu	to contain; to include
寒热	hán rè	cold and heat (TCM diagnosis)
寒山	Hán shān	Cold Mountain (Tang Chan poet)
韩非	Hán Fēi	Han Fei — greatest Legalist theorist, student of Xunzi — 韩非 (280–233 BCE): a prince of the state of Han who studied under Xunzi and became the most systematic theorist of Legalism 法家. His text, the 韩非子 Hánfēizǐ, synthesizes earlier Legalist thought and provides the fullest account of governance through law 法, technique 术, and power 势.
韩非子	Hán Fēi zǐ	Han Feizi; the Legalist theorist
汉服	Hàn fú	Traditional Han Dress
汉人	Hàn rén	Northern Chinese — third class — Former subjects of the Jin dynasty, including Han, Jurchen, Khitan, and Koreans. Conquered earlier, treated with somewhat less suspicion than southerners. Had somewhat greater access to lower official positions.
汉学	hàn xu é	Sinology; the study of Chinese language and civilization — 汉 Hàn (Chinese) + 学 xué (study, learning). The academic study of Chinese language, texts, and culture, especially as pursued by scholars outside China. A 汉学家 hànxuéjiā is a sinologist.
汉语	Hàn yǔ	Chinese (the language); the Han tongue — 汉 Hàn (the Han) + 语 yǔ (language, speech). The standard scholarly name for the Chinese language, framed by ethnicity rather than country. Its sister 中文 zhōngwén leans toward the written language, while 普通话 pǔtōnghuà names the standard Mandarin spoken form.
汉元素	hàn yuán sù	"Han elements" — commercially inspired garments that aren't structurally hànfú
汉族	Hàn zú	the Han ethnicity; the Han people — 汉 Hàn (of Han) + 族 zú (clan, ethnic group). The majority ethnic group of China, named after the Han dynasty. Set against the 少数民族 shǎoshù mínzú, the country's many minority peoples.
航天	háng tiān	Spaceflight — Spaceflight: "sailing the heavens," on the model of 航海 (seafaring). 航天员 is the official word for China's astronauts; the industry is 航天工程, space engineering.
毫不	háo bù	not at all, not in the least
好不好	hǎo bu hǎo	okay? — the agreement-seeking tag — Appended to a proposal, it softens a request into a negotiation: 我们明天再说，好不好? Its siblings 行不行 and 可不可以 climb the same ladder of politeness; children deploy 好不好 serially until granted.
好吃	hǎo chī	delicious
好处	hǎo chù	benefit; advantage; good point — 好 hǎo (good) + 处 chù (place; aspect; point). The good points of something: its advantages. Contrast: 坏处 huàichù (bad points; disadvantages).
好汉	hǎo hàn	a brave fellow; a stout-hearted hero — 好 hǎo (good) + 汉 hàn (fellow). A bold, admirable man, the heroic outlaw or stalwart of old novels. The saying 好汉不吃眼前亏 "a wise fellow does not take a loss he can avoid" shows its everyday use.
好喝	hǎo hē	tasty (to drink) — 好 (good) + 喝 (drink): pleasant to drink. Mandarin splits "tasty" by mouth-action, so liquids are 好喝 while solid food is 好吃 (hǎo chī), good to eat.
好看	hǎo kàn	good-looking; interesting to read
好了	hǎo le	done; ready; okay; alright (change of state) — 好 + 了 (change-of-state marker) = has become ready/done/okay. One of the most frequent two-syllable expressions in spoken Mandarin. Announces completion, signals readiness, and in reduplicated form (好了好了) means "alright, enough, okay."
好朋友	hǎo péng you	good friend; close friend — A close friend, the most common way to mark closeness. 好 hǎo (good) stacks directly in front. For an even closer bond there is 知己 zhījǐ, "one who knows me," a soulmate-friend who truly understands you, a notion with deep roots in classical Chinese friendship.
好奇	hào qí	curious; inquisitive — 好 hào (fond of) + 奇 qí (the strange, the unusual). Fond of the unusual , curious. 好奇心 hàoqíxīn = curiosity (the heart of fondness for strangeness).
好事成双	hǎo shì chéng shuāng	Good things come in pairs — Good things come in pairs: the principle that favors even numbers and pairing in gifts, decorations, and arrangements. Matched pairs of objects (candles, vases, figurines) are standard for weddings and auspicious occasions.
好听	hǎo tīng	pleasant-sounding; beautiful (of music, voice, speech) — 好 hǎo (good; well) + 听 (to listen). The auditory parallel of 好看 hǎokàn (nice-looking). Applied to music, voices, songs, and speech.
好闻	hǎo wén	nice to smell
好像	hǎo xiàng	seems like; as if
好兄弟	hǎo xiōng dì	good brothers — polite euphemism for wandering hungry ghosts — 好 hǎo (good) + 兄弟 xiōngdì (brothers). The Taiwanese term of respect used in place of 鬼 during Ghost Month. Addressing spirits as "good brothers" acknowledges their presence without the provocation of calling them ghosts; it is also an appeal to fraternal feeling.
喝酒	hē jiǔ	to drink alcohol — 喝 (drink) + 酒 (liquor). The default verb for drinking alcohol of any kind, from beer to baijiu. Central to banquet culture, where 喝酒 carries social obligation.
喝咖啡	hē kā fēi	to drink coffee — 喝 (drink) + 咖啡 (coffee). Like 喝茶, the phrase doubles as a soft social invitation: "改天一起喝咖啡" means let's meet up sometime.
喝水	hē shuǐ	to drink water — 喝 (drink) + 水 (water). The first sentence most learners build with this character, and the everyday reminder a Chinese host or parent will repeat constantly.
喝汤	hē tāng	to drink soup
喝完	hē wán	to finish drinking — 喝 (drink) + 完 (finished), another resultative: drink to completion, drain the glass. 完 marks the liquid as gone.
喝醉	hē zuì	to get drunk — 喝 (drink) + 醉 (drunk), a resultative compound: the 醉 is the result of the 喝. Often expanded to 喝醉了 (hē zuì le) for the completed state, "got drunk".
合并	hé bìng	to merge; to combine; to amalgamate — 合 hé (to join) + 并 bìng (to combine side by side). To merge separate units into one: 两个部门合并了 "the two departments merged." Common in business, administration, and biology.
合唱	hé chàng	chorus; to sing in chorus — 合 hé (to join, to combine) + 唱 chàng (to sing). Voices joined into one: a chorus, or the act of singing together. 合唱团 héchàngtuán is a choir. Contrast with 独唱 dúchàng, a solo, where one voice stands alone.
合而为一	hé ér wéi yī	to merge into one; to unite into a single whole — 合 hé (to combine; to close) + 而 ér (and then; thereby) + 为 wéi (become) + 一. A classical four-character phrase describing the merging of separate things into wholeness. Used in philosophy, business mergers, artistic synthesis, and descriptions of cultural fusion.
合法	hé fǎ	lawful; legal; legitimate — 合 hé (to fit; to accord with) + 法 fǎ (law). In accordance with the law, on the right side of the line: 合法权利 "lawful rights." Its opposite is 非法 fēifǎ (illegal) or 违法 wéifǎ (to break the law).
合理	hé lǐ	reasonable; rational; sound — 合 hé (to accord with) + 理 lǐ (reason; principle). Something that accords with reason or fairness: 合理的价格 "a reasonable price," 合理安排 "a sensible arrangement." Its opposite is 不合理.
合上	hé shàng	to close (a book, lid, door); to shut — 合 hé (to close) + 上 shàng (a result complement: shut to). To bring two edges together so a thing is closed: 合上书 "close the book," 合上盖子 "put the lid on."
合适	hé shì	suitable; appropriate; fitting — 合 hé (to fit) + 适 shì (to be apt). An adjective describing something that fits the situation well: 合适的工作 "a suitable job," 大小合适 "the right size." Its opposite is 不合适.
合同	hé tong	Contract — Contract. 签合同 (qiān hétong) is to sign one. The parties inside it, 甲方 and 乙方 (Party A and Party B), have become everyday slang for client and vendor.
合作	hé zuò	to cooperate; to collaborate; cooperation — 合 hé (to join) + 作 zuò (to do; to work). To join efforts and work as one. The everyday word for partnership of every scale, from two classmates to two nations: 合作伙伴 "a cooperation partner."
和平	hé píng	peace; peaceful — 和 hé (harmony) + 平 píng (calm). Peace as the opposite of war and conflict: 世界和平 "world peace," 和平谈判 "peace talks," 和平相处 "to live together in peace."
和氏璧	Hé Shì Bì	the legendary jade disc
和谐	hé xié	harmony; harmonious
河岸	hé àn	riverbank
河边	hé biān	riverside; riverbank — 河 (river) + 边 (biān, edge / side). The strip of ground along a river, where people walk, fish, and wash. One of the first place-words a beginner builds with 河.
河灯	hé dēng	river lanterns
河流	hé liú	river; watercourse — 河 (river) + 流 (liú, to flow / a current). The generic technical term for a river as a flowing body of water, the word a geography textbook or a news report reaches for.
河水	hé shuǐ	river water — 河 (river) + 水 (shuǐ, water). The water of a river as a substance, distinct from sea water 海水 (hǎishuǐ) or rain. Common in the proverb about minding your own affairs.
河套	Hé tào	The Great Bend — The Great Bend: the large northern loop of the Yellow River into Inner Mongolia. The fertile irrigated land within the bend has been farmed since antiquity and was historically a zone of contest between Han Chinese settlers and nomadic peoples of the steppe.
核心	hé xīn	core; nucleus; the essential center — 核 hé (nucleus; kernel; seed, as in 核武器 nuclear weapons) + 心 xīn (heart). The innermost, hardest, most essential center. Where 中心 is the geometric center, 核心 is the conceptual kernel, what everything else wraps around. Heavily used in political discourse, business strategy, and academic writing.
荷包蛋	hé bāo dàn	fried egg, poached egg
黑暗	hēi àn	darkness; (figuratively) corrupt, benighted — 黑 hēi (black) + 暗 àn (dim; dark; unlit). Physical darkness, and by easy extension moral or social darkness: an age, a society, or a regime described as 黑暗 is corrupt and oppressive, a world where justice cannot be seen. 旧社会很黑暗 (the old society was full of darkness) is a stock phrase.
黑板	hēi bǎn	blackboard; chalkboard — 黑 hēi (black) + 板 bǎn (board; plank). The classroom blackboard, a high-frequency HSK word and a useful anchor for the plain color sense.
黑客	hēi kè	hacker — 黑 hēi (black; illicit) + 客 kè (guest; visitor). The standard Chinese word for a hacker. It works on two levels at once: phonetically 黑客 echoes the English "hacker," and semantically it reads as "black guest," the uninvited intruder who enters a system in the dark.
黑马	hēi mǎ	dark horse; an unexpected winner — 黑 hēi (black; dark) + 马 mǎ (horse). A direct calque of the English "dark horse," now fully naturalized: a competitor nobody expected who surges from obscurity to win. Used constantly in sports, elections, business, and entertainment competitions.
黑色	hēi sè	the color black — 黑 hēi + 色 sè (color). The neutral word for black as a color. Unlike its English counterpart, black in classical Chinese cosmology was not purely negative: in the 五行 (Five Phases) system, black is the color of Water, of the North, of winter, and of the kidneys, a respectable and necessary part of the…
黑市	hēi shì	black market — 黑 hēi (illicit; off the books) + 市 shì (market). A market operating outside legal channels, where goods or currency change hands at unofficial prices.
很多	hěn duō	a lot; many
很多 · 不少	hěn duō bù shǎo	many, a lot: plain and understated — 很多 is the everyday "many, a lot": 很多人 (many people), 很多问题 (many questions). 不少, literally "not few", says the same thing through understatement, and often carries a note of mild surprise: 他认识不少人 (he knows quite a few people).
红白喜事	hóng bái xǐ shì	weddings and funerals
红包	hóng bāo	red envelope; a cash gift; a digital money packet — 红 hóng + 包 bāo (packet; bundle). The red paper envelope filled with money, given to children at New Year, to a couple at their wedding, and to employees as a bonus. The red is the point: the envelope blesses the money inside.
红灯	hóng dēng	red light (traffic) — 红 (hóng, red) + 灯 (light): the red traffic signal that means stop. Here 灯 has shed every trace of flame and means pure colored signal. Its partner is 绿灯 (lǜdēng), the green light to go.
红火	hóng huo	flourishing; thriving; booming and lively — 红 hóng (red; auspicious) + 火 huǒ (fire). A business, a celebration, or a neighborhood that is 红火 is booming, bustling, full of warmth and prosperity. The image fuses the lucky red with the energy of fire: a scene so lively it glows.
红巾军	Hóng jīn jūn	Red Turban Army
红军	Hóng jūn	the Red Army; the early Chinese Communist military force — 红 hóng (red; revolutionary) + 军 jūn (army). The Chinese Workers' and Peasants' Red Army, the Communist fighting force from 1927 until it was renamed in the late 1930s.
红脸	hóng liǎn	to blush; to flush red (with anger or embarrassment) — 红 (red) + 脸 (face). The cheeks going red, whether from shyness or from a flare of temper. 脸红 (liǎnhóng) reverses the order for the same blush; 红脸 also names the stern, blunt role someone plays in a good-cop bad-cop pairing.
红绿灯	hóng lǜ dēng	traffic light (literally "red-green light") — 红 hóng (red) + 绿 lǜ (green) + 灯 dēng (light; lamp). The everyday word for a traffic signal, built from its two decisive colors with red named first. 闯红灯 (chuǎng hóngdēng) is to run a red light, a common phrase that has also become a metaphor for breaking a rule that everyone agrees on.
红人	hóng rén	a favored person; someone in favor with the powerful; a star — 红 hóng (popular; in favor) + 人 rén (person). Someone basking in favor, whether a boss's trusted lieutenant, a court favorite, or a rising celebrity. The metaphor runs straight from the color: to be 红 is to be celebrated, so a 红人 is a person bathed in that good fortune.
红色	hóng sè	the color red; (figuratively) revolutionary, Communist — 红 hóng + 色 sè (color). The neutral term for red as a color, but watch the second sense: 红色 also means "revolutionary" or "Communist" in political contexts. 红色旅游 (red tourism) is travel to revolutionary historical sites; 红色经典 (red classics) are the canonical works of revolutionary culture.
红十字	Hóng Shí zì	the Red Cross — 红 hóng (red) + 十字 shízì (cross). The International Committee of the Red Cross, established 1863. The Chinese translation renders the emblem directly: the red 十-shaped cross. The full institutional name is 红十字国际委员会 Hóng Shízì Guójì Wěiyuánhuì.
红卫兵	Hóng wèi bīng	Red Guards
红线	hóng xiàn	red thread of fate; also: a line that must not be crossed — 红 hóng (red , the color of luck, celebration, and vital force in Chinese culture) + 线 xiàn (thread; line). The red thread of fate in romantic mythology.
后方	hòu fāng	the rear; behind the front line — 后 (rear) + 方 (direction; side; region). The military and formal register word for the rear area behind a front line or forward position. Contrasted with 前方 qiánfāng (the front, the forward area). Also used metaphorically for the support structure behind any endeavor: family, logistics, home base.
后宫	hòu gōng	the imperial harem; the rear palace — 后 (rear; and the empress sense) + 宫 (palace). The residential complex at the back of the imperial enclosure housing the emperor's wives and concubines. The term plays on both senses of 后: the rear palace (spatially, at the back) and the palace of the 后 (the empress and her female hierarchy).
后来	hòu lái	afterward; later; subsequently — 后 (after) + 来 (to come; to arrive). "What came after" , subsequent events in a narrative or sequence. 后来 always refers to the past relative to a reference point already established: you set the scene, then 后来 picks up the story.
后门	hòu mén	back door; backdoor; unofficial channel — 后 (rear) + 门 (door; gate). Literally the door at the back of a building. Figuratively: an unofficial, informal, or improper channel for getting something done.
后面	hòu miàn	behind; at the back; in the rear — 后 (behind) + 面 (face; side; direction). The most common spatial word for "behind" in everyday Mandarin. Used for physical position relative to a reference point. Interchangeable with 后边 hòubian in many contexts; 后面 is slightly more formal.
后天	hòu tiān	the day after tomorrow — 后 hòu (after; behind; later) + 天 tiān (day). Chinese has dedicated words for two days in either direction of today: 前天 qiántiān / 昨天 zuótiān / 今天 jīntiān / 明天 míngtiān / 后天 hòutiān. Beyond that, you count: 三天后 sān tiān hòu (three days later).
后者	hòu zhě	the latter
候车	hòu chē	to wait for a bus or train — 候 hòu (to wait) + 车 chē (vehicle). To wait for transport. Seen everywhere on station signage: 候车室 hòuchēshì "waiting room," 候车厅 "waiting hall."
候鸟	hòu niǎo	migratory bird — 候 hòu (season) + 鸟 niǎo (bird). The bird that moves with the season, contrasted with 留鸟 liúniǎo, the resident bird that stays year-round.
候诊	hòu zhěn	to wait to see a doctor — 候 hòu (to wait) + 诊 zhěn (to examine medically). To wait for a medical consultation. The room where patients sit is the 候诊室 hòuzhěnshì.
忽然	hū rán	suddenly; all at once — 忽 hū (sudden, to neglect) + 然 rán (-ly). Marks an event arriving without warning: 忽然下雨 "it suddenly began to rain." Usually placed before the verb. Near-synonym of 突然.
胡同	hú tòng	hutong, alley
湖笔	hú bǐ	Huzhou brush , brushes made in Huzhou, Zhejiang province, regarded as the finest in China. The term is used as a quality designation, much as "Huizhou ink" signals quality in the ink category. Huzhou brush-making has been a continuous craft tradition for over a thousand years.
湖面	hú miàn	surface of a lake — 湖 (lake) plus 面 (surface): the open face of the water, the plane that mirrors sky and mountain.
湖南	Hún án	Hunan province — 湖 (lake) plus 南 (south): the province south of Dongting Lake, known for spicy cuisine and as Mao Zedong's birthplace.
湖畔	hú pàn	lakeside, the shore — 湖 (lake) plus 畔 (pàn, bank, edge): the land at the water's rim, a literary word for a lakeshore walk or dwelling.
湖泊	hú pō	lakes (collective) — 湖 (lake) plus 泊 (pō, a still pool). The pairing is the standard geographic term for lakes taken as a class, the word a textbook or atlas uses.
湖水	hú shuǐ	lake water — 湖 (lake) plus 水 (water): the water of a lake itself, often invoked for its stillness and color.
互相	hù xiāng	mutually; each other; one another — 互 hù (mutual) + 相 xiāng (each other). Placed before a verb, it says the action runs in both directions between two or more parties: 互相帮助 "to help each other," 互相理解 "to understand one another."
户外	hù wài	outdoors; in the open air — 户 hù (door; household) + 外 wài (outside). Literally "outside the door," the open air beyond a building. Common in 户外运动 "outdoor sports" and 户外活动 "outdoor activities."
花白	huā bái	grizzled; salt-and-pepper (of hair) — 花 huā (mixed-color) + 白 bái (white). Of hair or a beard, mixed black and white, grizzled: 花白的头发 "grizzled hair." The "many-colored" sense of 花 applied to the heads of the middle-aged.
花旦	huā dàn	lively female role
花朵	huā duǒ	flower; bloom (as a discrete blossom) — 花 huā (flower) + 朵 duǒ (the measure word for blooms). A flower viewed as an individual blossom, slightly more literary than bare 花. Children are often called 祖国的花朵, "the flowers of the motherland."
花费	huā fèi	to spend; expense; cost — 花 huā (to spend) + 费 fèi (fee, to expend). A more formal word for spending, and also a noun meaning the expense itself: 生活花费 "living expenses." Heavier and more written than bare 花.
花甲	huā jiǎ	60th birthday; one full cycle
花椒	huā jiāo	Sichuan pepper; prickly ash berry — Sichuan pepper , the dried husk of the prickly ash berry, not related to black pepper or chili pepper. Contains hydroxy-alpha sanshool, which activates the same receptor as light touch and low-frequency vibration, producing the characteristic tingling numbness on the tongue and lips.
花轿	huā jiào	The decorated red sedan chair in which the bride was traditionally carried to the wedding. The chair was ornately painted and curtained, surrounded by musicians and attendants. The tradition has been revived in some contemporary weddings as a heritage element.
花开	huā kāi	flowers bloom
花钱	huā qián	to spend money — 花 huā (to spend) + 钱 qián (money). The basic verb-object phrase for spending money. Often split by an amount: 花了不少钱 "spent quite a lot of money," 花很多钱 "spend a lot."
花时间	huā shí jiān	to spend time; to take time — 花 huā (to spend) + 时间 shíjiān (time). To spend or take time on something. The same verb 花 covers time as well as money, where English needs a different word for each.
花样	huā yàng	pattern; variety; fancy trick — 花 huā (patterned) + 样 yàng (shape, kind). A decorative pattern or a fancy variation, and by extension a trick or gimmick: 玩花样 "to play tricks," 花样滑冰 "figure skating."
花园	huā yuán	garden — 花 huā (flower) + 园 yuán (garden, plot). A garden, a cultivated plot of flowers and plants. 植物园 zhíwùyuán is a botanical garden; 动物园 dòngwùyuán, a zoo, keeps the same 园.
华人	huá rén	ethnic Chinese (esp. diaspora)
华文	Huá wén	overseas Chinese usage
滑雪	huá xu ě	to ski; skiing — 滑 huá (to slide; slippery) + 雪 xuě (snow). To ski, literally "to slide on snow." The word covers downhill skiing and snowboarding alike in casual use.
划分	huà fēn	to demarcate; to divide
画画	huà huà	to draw; to paint — 画 huà (to draw) + 画 huà (a picture). A verb-object pair, literally "to draw a drawing," the everyday way to say one is drawing or painting. The first 画 is the action, the second is what it produces.
画家	huà jiā	painter; visual artist — 画 huà (to paint; to draw; a painting) + 家 jiā. One who has made painting their home. The 家 suffix elevates the painter from a craftsperson to a master, implying sustained creative commitment and a body of work rather than mere technical skill.
画面	huà miàn	a scene; an image (on a screen or in a picture); a frame — 画 huà (picture) + 面 miàn (surface; face). The visible surface of a picture or screen: the framed image you see in a film, a photo, or a painting. 画面很美 "the image is beautiful."
话说回来	huà shuō huí lái	"Coming back to what we were saying" or "on the other hand — "Coming back to what we were saying" or "on the other hand." Marks a return to a previous thread, often with a contrasting take. Useful for acknowledging a counterpoint to your own argument: "but coming back to it, you do have a point that…"
话题	huà tí	topic; subject of conversation — 话 huà (speech; talk) + 题 tí (topic). What a conversation is about: 换个话题 "change the subject," 热门话题 "a hot topic." It frames the heading of talk rather than writing.
怀春	huái chūn	to long for love
淮扬菜	Huái yáng Cài	Huaiyang cuisine — Huaiyang cuisine: the specific tradition of the Yangzhou-Huai'an axis, considered the most technically refined of Chinese culinary traditions and the foundation of state banquet cooking.
坏处	huài chu	harm, drawback, downside — 坏 (bad) + 处 (point, aspect). The disadvantage or harm of something, the standard counterpart to 好处 (hǎochu, benefit). Weighed up when deciding whether something is worth doing.
坏蛋	huài dàn	scoundrel, bad egg — 坏 (bad) + 蛋 (egg). A literal rotten egg, used figuratively for a scoundrel. Colloquial and elastic: a genuine insult when angry, a flirtatious tease between a couple when said with a smile.
坏了	huài le	broken; out of order — 坏 (to break down) + 了 (change-of-state particle). The everyday way to say a device or appliance has stopped working, or that food has gone off. Also a spoken exclamation: 坏了！ "Uh-oh, this is bad!"
坏人	huài rén	bad person, villain — 坏 (bad) + 人 (person). The default word a child learns for the villain in a story or the stranger to avoid. Broad and moral, covering everyone from the cartoon antagonist to a genuine criminal.
欢呼	huān hū	to cheer; to hail with joy — 欢 huān (joy) + 呼 hū (to shout; to call). To shout for joy, the sound a crowd makes in celebration: 欢呼声 "cheers," 人们欢呼雀跃 "people cheered and jumped for joy."
欢快	huān kuài	lively; cheerful; brisk and light-hearted — 欢 huān (joy) + 快 kuài (fast; quick). Joy with a quick, light tempo: a brisk and cheerful mood. Often said of music, rhythm, and movement: 欢快的旋律 "a lively melody," 欢快的舞步 "brisk, cheerful dance steps."
欢乐	huān lè	joy; merriment; happy and festive — 欢 huān (joy) + 乐 lè (happy; glad). Joy of the shared, festive kind: a celebration, a holiday, a crowd in good spirits. 欢乐的气氛 "a joyful atmosphere." More outward and communal than the quiet 高兴.
欢送	huān sòng	to give a warm send-off — 欢 (joyful) plus 送 (see off). To send someone off with celebration: a farewell party for a retiring colleague or a graduating class. The opposite number of 欢迎 (huānyíng), to welcome.
欢腾	huān téng	to be jubilant; to seethe with joy — 欢 huān (joy) + 腾 téng (to leap; to surge). A crowd boiling over with joy, leaping and shouting. Said of stadiums, squares, and whole towns at a great moment: 全场欢腾 "the whole place erupted in jubilation."
欢喜	huān xǐ	happy; joyful; delighted — 欢 huān (merry) + 喜 xǐ (glad). A warm, somewhat colloquial word for being happy and pleased, common in the south and in the phrase 欢欢喜喜 "all merry and glad," and in 皆大欢喜 "everyone happy."
欢迎	huān yíng	to welcome; welcome! — 欢 huān (joy) + 迎 yíng (to greet; to meet). To receive someone gladly. Also the set greeting 欢迎 "welcome!" and the shop sign 欢迎光临 "welcome, please come in." 受欢迎 shòu huānyíng means "popular; well-liked."
环节	huán jié	link; step in a process
幻想	huàn xiǎng	fantasy; illusion; to fantasize — 幻 huàn (illusion; phantom; to change; to vanish) + 想 xiǎng (thought; imagination). Thinking directed at what is illusory or impossible: fantasy, wishful thinking, daydreaming. Carries a mildly critical register , implies the thought has decoupled from reality.
换班	huàn bān	to change shifts — 换 (swap) + 班 (shift). To trade work shifts with a coworker, or for one shift to hand over to the next. The swap is of time slots rather than objects.
换车	huàn chē	to transfer (vehicles) — 换 (swap) + 车 (vehicle). To get off one bus, train, or subway line and board another. The word every commuter says at an interchange station; 换乘 (huànchéng) is the signboard version.
换乘	huàn chéng	to transfer (transit)
换个话题	huàn ge huà tí	"Let's change the subject — "Let's change the subject." Direct topic-change marker, used when the current thread has run its course or when you want to move on from something uncomfortable. Slightly more deliberate than 对了; signals an intentional shift rather than a stumble onto something new.
换句话说	huàn jù huà shuō	in other words — Literally swap a sentence and say it: 换 (swap) + 句话 (a sentence) + 说 (say). A fixed connective that restates a point another way, exactly like English "to put it differently."
换钱	huàn qián	to change money — 换 (swap) + 钱 (money). A verb-object pair for converting currency or breaking a large note into small ones. The default phrase at any bank counter or exchange window.
换衣服	huàn yī fu	to change clothes — 换 (swap) + 衣服 (clothes). The everyday house phrase: take off what you are wearing and put on something else. The same frame builds 换鞋 (change shoes) and 换床单 (change the sheets).
皇后	huáng hòu	empress; empress consort — 皇 huáng (august; emperor) + 后 (the commanding consort). The principal wife of a reigning emperor in the Chinese imperial system. A formal title with specific ritual privileges and a defined role in the imperial hierarchy.
黄帝	Huáng dì	the Yellow Emperor
黄帝内经	Huáng dì Nèi jīng	Yellow Emperor's Classic of Internal Medicine
黄河	huáng hé	the Yellow River — 黄 (huáng, yellow) + 河 (river). The silt-yellow river of north China, cradle of early Chinese civilization. Here 河 returns to its oldest job, naming this exact river, with 黄 added once 河 stopped meaning it by default.
黄昏	huáng hūn	dusk, twilight
黄了	huáng le	to fall through, to fail
黄牛	huáng niú	ox; ticket scalper — 黄 (huáng, yellow) + 牛, literally the yellow ox of the fields. In cities it became slang for a ticket scalper, from the way scalpers swarm and jostle like a milling herd.
黄色	huáng sè	yellow (the color); obscene
黄油	huáng yóu	butter
回报	huí bào	to repay; a return, reward — 回 (huí, to return) plus 报 (to requite): to give back in kind. Used for repaying kindness and, in finance, for the return on an investment.
回答	huí dá	to answer; to reply; an answer — 回 huí (back) + 答 dá (to respond). To send a response back to a question: 回答问题 "to answer a question." As a noun, the answer itself: 一个满意的回答 "a satisfactory answer."
回到刚才的话题	huí dào gāng cái de huà tí	"Coming back to what we were talking about — "Coming back to what we were talking about." Returns to an earlier thread after a digression. Particularly useful in longer conversations where you want to close out a tangent and pick up the original point.
回复	huí fù	to reply
回甘	huí gān	returning sweetness — the lingering aftertaste
回锅肉	huí guō ròu	Twice-Cooked Pork
回家	huí jiā	to go home; to return home — 回 huí (to return) + 家 jiā (home). One of the most common V+location phrases in the language. The directional verb 回 indicates movement back toward an origin point: home as the natural destination of return, not merely a place you happen to go.
回来	huí lái	to come back; to return — 回 huí (to return; to turn back) + 来 lái (toward here). To turn and come back toward the speaker's reference point, usually home, a shared location, or where you were before leaving. The counterpart is 回去 huíqù (to go back, returning away from the speaker).
回去	huí qù	to go back
回头	huí tóu	to turn one's head; later; to repent — 回 huí (turn back) + 头 tóu (head). To turn the head around, and by extension "in a little while" (回头见 "see you later") or to turn back from a wrong path (回头是岸 "to repent is to reach the shore").
回向	huí xiàng	merit transfer to others
回忆	huí yì	to recall; to look back on; a memory — 回 huí (turn back) + 忆 yì (to remember). To turn the mind back to the past. As a noun, a remembered scene: 美好的回忆 "fond memories." The eddy here loops through time rather than space.
回应	huí yìng	to reply; to respond
汇报	huì bào	to report to superiors
会说中文	huì shuō Zhōng wén	can speak Chinese — learned through study and practice — 会 indicates that a skill was acquired through learning, training, or practice. The speaker once did not have this ability and now does , through effort. This is the fundamental meaning: know how to.
会议纪要	huì yì jì yào	meeting minutes — A short written summary of decisions, owners, and dates, sent the same day. Offering to write it yourself means your version becomes the record. 白纸黑字 (báizhǐ hēizì, "black ink on white paper") is the idiom for having it in writing.
绘画	huì huà	painting; drawing (as an art form) — 绘 huì (to depict) + 画 huà (to paint). A more formal word than 画画, naming painting as a discipline or art form: 绘画艺术 "the art of painting." Used in writing and in talk about fine art rather than casual sketching.
慧可	Huì kě	Huike — second patriarch; the arm in the snow — Huike (487–593 CE) is remembered for two things: the snow-and-arm story of his fierce aspiration, and the exchange above about the unfindable mind , which is itself a kōan on the non-existence of a substantial, locatable self.
慧远	Huì yuǎn	Huiyuan (334–416 CE) — Eastern Jin dynasty founder — Huiyuan is revered as the first patriarch of Chinese Pure Land Buddhism, though he did not use that term himself.
混蛋	hún dàn	bastard, scoundrel
活动	huó dòng	activity; event; to move about; to exercise — 活 huó (lively) + 动 dòng (to move). As a noun, an organized activity or event; as a verb, to move one's body, to stretch and stir after sitting still. The lively-and-moving pair names anything that gets people up and going.
活儿	huó r	work; a job; a task; a piece of work — 活 huó (work) + the retroflex 儿 (ér) ending, typical of northern colloquial speech. The concrete job itself: the work to be done, often skilled handwork. A fine piece of craftsmanship is 好活儿 (good work).
活力	huó lì	vitality; vigor
活泼	huó po	lively; vivacious; animated — 活 huó (lively) + 泼 pō (to splash, itself a water word). A doubled water image, the splash of a living current, describing a bright, spirited, outgoing temperament. Most natural for children and young people: 活泼可爱 (lively and adorable).
活菩萨	huó pú sà	living bodhisattva; a living saint — 活 huó = living, alive. Used in folk speech to describe a person of extraordinary virtue or generosity , a doctor who heals the poor without payment, a neighbor who gives without being asked.
活着	huó zhe	to be alive; living — 活 huó (to live) + 着 zhe (the durative marker, an ongoing state). To be in the state of being alive, as opposed to 死了 (dead). Yú Huá's famous novel 《活着》 takes the word as its whole subject: simply going on living through everything.
火车	huǒ chē	train (lit. "fire cart") — 火 huǒ + 车 chē (vehicle; cart). Named when steam locomotives first arrived in China , "fire-powered cart." The word has outlasted the steam era. 高铁 gāotiě (high-speed rail) is now preferred for bullet trains, but 火车 remains in universal use.
火车站	huǒ chē zhàn	railway station
火锅	huǒ guō	hotpot; steamboat — 火 huǒ + 锅 guō (pot; wok). A simmering communal pot at the table , the quintessential Chinese social meal. 吃火锅 = to eat hotpot, one of the most common social dining activities. Regional varieties: 四川火锅 Sichuan hotpot (numbing-spicy 麻辣), 北京涮羊肉 Beijing lamb hotpot (clear broth).
火候	huǒ hou	the right heat and timing; (by extension) the right moment or level of skill — 火 huǒ (fire) + 候 hou (the timing of). In cooking, the exact control of heat and duration that makes or ruins a dish. Figuratively, the ripeness of a moment or the maturity of a skill: 火候到了 "the moment is ripe."
火山	huǒ shān	volcano — 火 huǒ (fire) + 山 shān (mountain). A fire-mountain. Strikingly direct. Related: 火山爆发 volcanic eruption · 活火山 active volcano · 死火山 extinct volcano · 休眠火山 dormant volcano.
火星	huǒ xīng	Mars; a spark — The fire star twice over: the red planet of the five-phase planet names, and the everyday spark from a fire or a grinder's wheel. Internet slang added a third life: a 火星人 (Martian) is someone hopelessly behind on the news.
火焰	huǒ yàn	flame; blaze — 火 huǒ + 焰 yàn (flame , itself contains the fire radical 火). Used for actual flames and for intensity: 熊熊火焰 "roaring flames." Also in compound idioms: 火焰般的热情 "flame-like passion."
货船	huò chuán	cargo ship
货物	huò wù	goods; cargo; merchandise — 货 huò (goods; commodity) + 物 wù (thing). Goods as freight or stock in trade, the things that move through commerce: 运送货物 "to transport goods," 货物清单 "a manifest of cargo."
肌肉	jī ròu	muscle — 肌 (jī, muscle tissue) + 肉 (flesh). The fibrous flesh that moves the body; both characters carry the 月 flesh-radical.
鸡蛋	jī dàn	chicken egg — 鸡 (jī, chicken) + 蛋 (egg): the default egg in Chinese kitchens. When a recipe or menu just says egg, it means a hen's egg, so 鸡蛋 is one of the first nouns most learners cook with.
鸡肉	jī ròu	chicken (meat) — 鸡 (chicken) + 肉 (ròu, meat). The flesh on the menu, as opposed to the live bird. Pair it with 牛肉 (niúròu, beef) and 猪肉 (zhūròu, pork) to name the everyday meats.
基本	jī běn	basic; fundamental; (基本上) basically, on the whole — 基 jī (foundation) + 本 běn (root). The base layer of something: 基本功 jīběngōng (basic skills), 基本工资 (base salary). The adverb 基本上 jīběnshàng means "basically, by and large."
基督教	Jī dū jiào	Christianity
基因	jī yīn	Gene — Gene: sounds like the English, means "basic cause." 转基因 (zhuǎnjīyīn, transgenic/GMO) is the charged supermarket word built on it.
激动	jī dòng	excited; agitated; stirred up; worked up — 激 jī (to rouse; to surge) + 动 dòng (to stir). A fast, strong emotional stirring, joy, anger, or agitation that one can barely sit still through: 太激动了 "so excited," 别激动 "calm down."
及第	jí dì	to pass the imperial examination; to place in the rankings — 及 jí (to reach; to attain) + 第 dì (rank). To succeed in the civil-service exam and be placed in the official order. A historical and literary term; the modern equivalent is 考上 kǎoshàng (to test into).
极乐	jí lè	supreme bliss; the Pure Land
急忙	jí máng	hurriedly, in a rush — 急 (urgent) + 忙 (máng, busy). An adverb of manner: doing something fast because time is short. It sits right before the verb to color the action with haste.
急诊	jí zhěn	emergency medical treatment, the ER — 急 (urgent) + 诊 (zhěn, to diagnose). Emergency consultation. 急诊 is the hospital department you go to when something cannot wait, the Chinese counterpart of the emergency room. 看急诊 (kàn jízhěn) means to go to the ER.
疾病	jí bìng	disease, illness (formal) — 疾 (jí) and 病 are near-synonyms paired into one formal noun. In classical usage 疾 was the slight ailment and 病 the serious one; fused together, 疾病 is the neutral cover term for disease in medical and official language.
集体	jí tǐ	collective; group; the community — 集 jí (to gather) + 体 tǐ (body). A gathered body of people acting as one: 集体活动 "a group activity," 集体主义 "collectivism." Set against the individual, 个人 gèrén.
几 + 量词	jǐ + M W	a few, a handful (under ten) — Outside questions, 几 + measure word means "a few": 几个朋友 (a few friends), 几本书 (a few books), 前几天 (a few days ago). The same word asks "how many?" when the expected answer is small; the statement and question readings are told apart by context and intonation.
几个	jǐ ge	a few; several; how many — 几 jǐ (how many; a few) + 个 ge. Either a question ("how many?") or a vague small number ("a few"), depending on context: 几个人 "how many people?" or "a few people."
己见	jǐ jiàn	one's own view; personal opinion — 己 jǐ (one's own) + 见 jiàn (view). The opinion that is distinctly one's own, valued for independence but cautioned against when it hardens into obstinacy. Lives in 固执己见, "to cling stubbornly to one's own view."
己所不欲	jǐ suǒ bù yù	what one does not desire (for oneself)
记笔记	jì bǐ jì	to take notes — 记 jì (to record) + 笔记 bǐjì (notes). The standard phrase for taking notes: in class, in a meeting, during a lecture. The verb-object construction: 上课要记笔记 (take notes in class).
记得	jì de	to remember; to recall — 记 jì (to keep in mind) + 得 de (a resultative marker). To have something still held in memory, the everyday word for remembering: 我记得他 "I remember him." Its opposite is 忘记 wàngjì (to forget).
记号	jì hào	a mark; a sign (made to remember by) — 记 jì (to mark) + 号 hào (sign, number). A mark made deliberately so you can find or recall a spot: 做记号 "to make a mark." Often a tick beside a line or a fold in a page.
记录	jì lù	to record; a record; minutes — 记 jì (to write down) + 录 lù (to copy, to register). To set something down in writing, and the written result itself. Also the "record" that is broken in sport: 世界记录 "world record."
记忆	jì yì	memory; to remember (formal) — 记 jì (to remember) + 忆 yì (to recall, recollect). The faculty of memory and its contents, more formal and abstract than 记得: 童年的记忆 "childhood memories," 记忆力 jìyìlì "the power of memory."
记者	jì zhě	reporter; journalist — 记 jì (to record) + 者 zhě (one who). Literally "one who records," the reporter whose work is to set down and report events: 新闻记者 "a news reporter."
记住	jì zhù	to remember firmly; to commit to memory — 记 jì (to keep in mind) + 住 zhù (a resultative meaning "to stay fast"). To make something stick so it will not slip away: 记住这个号码 "memorize this number." The 住 stresses that the memory has caught and held.
忌日	jì rì	death anniversary (ritual day)
季节	jì jié	season
既然	jì rán	since; now that
祭天	jì tiān	offering to Heaven
稷下学宫	Jì xià Xu é gōng	Jixia Academy — the Warring States research university — Established by the state of Qi near its capital Linzi (临淄 Líncí) in the early 4th century BCE, operating for about a century. Scholars from all schools received state stipends and official titles without bureaucratic duties.
加工	jiā gōng	to process; to manufacture
加快	jiā kuài	to accelerate; to speed up — 加 jiā (to add; to increase) + 快 kuài (fast). Adding more speed to something already in motion. Common in official and news language for policy goals, construction schedules, and economic development; equally at home in everyday speech.
加热	jiā rè	to heat up — 加 (jiā, to add) plus 热 (heat): to add heat, to warm something up. The verb on the microwave button and the recipe step.
加上	jiā shàng	in addition; plus; adding to this
加微信	jiā Wēi xìn	to add on WeChat — 扫一扫 (sǎo yi sǎo) is "scan"; one person shows their QR code (二维码 èrwéimǎ), the other scans. 我扫你 means "I'll scan yours." 拉群 (lā qún) is to create a group chat, the norm for an order with several people on each side.
加油站	jiā yóu zhàn	petrol station — 加油 (jiāyóu, to add fuel) + 站 (station): the place a car stops to refuel. Note that 加油 doubles as the cheer "let's go! keep it up!", literally "add oil," but here it is strictly the fuel sense.
夹菜	jiā cài	To pick food for someone else , placing food from a shared dish into another person's bowl as a gesture of care and hospitality. The action is performed with chopsticks and directed toward honored guests, elders, or someone whose preferences you are attending to.
佳节	jiā jié	auspicious festival; festive holiday — literary register — 佳 jiā (excellent; fine; auspicious) + 节 jié (festival; season; node). A slightly elevated term for a holiday or festival occasion, preferred in literary and formal contexts over the more neutral 节日 (jiérì).
佳音	jiā yīn	good news; welcome tidings — 佳 jiā (fine; good) + 音 yīn (word; news). The good news one hopes for, slightly formal in tone: 静候佳音 "to await good news quietly," a polite way to say you are hoping for a favorable reply.
家常	jiā cháng	everyday home life; homestyle; ordinary — 家 jiā (home) + 常 cháng (ordinary; often; constant). The routine of domestic life: meals, small talk, everyday rhythms. A warmly neutral word that positions the homely and ordinary as valuable rather than inferior.
家规	jiā guī	family rules; household rules — 家 jiā (family) + 规 guī (rules; regulations; norms). The explicit code of conduct of a household, the spoken version of what 家风 transmits implicitly. Distinguished families in imperial China often had written 家规 codifying expectations for children's conduct, study, and marriage.
家国情怀	jiā guó qíng huái	family-country sentiment; patriotic feeling rooted in personal bonds — 家 (family) + 国 (country) + 情怀 qínghuái (feeling, sentiment, cherished aspiration). The emotional bond that flows from family love outward to love of country: the Confucian ladder enacted as feeling.
家祭	jiā jì	domestic ancestral offering
家谱	jiā pǔ	genealogy book
家人	jiā rén	family members; one's family — 家 jiā + 人 rén (person/people). The people of the home. More intimate and emotionally warm than the formal compound 家庭成员 (family members, bureaucratic). 家人 feels like "my people": those who share your roof and your life.
家庭	jiā tíng	Family; household — Family; household. The nuclear unit of parents and children who share a home. 家庭作业 (jiātíng zuòyè, homework , lit. "family work") and 家庭医生 (jiātíng yīshēng, family doctor) show the compound in everyday use. The term carries a warmth and domesticity that the more formal 家族 lacks.
家乡	jiā xiāng	hometown; native place — 家 jiā + 乡 xiāng (countryside; native place, the rural origin point). The place where 家 was first made. A place of origin rather than merely an address: family roots, ancestral connections, formative landscape. Central to Chinese identity, deeply tied to belonging and longing.
家训	jiā xùn	family precepts; ancestral rules
家园	jiā yuán	home; homeland; one's native place
家长	jiā zhǎng	head of household; guardian
家族	jiā zú	Clan; extended family network; lineage group sharing a surname. 家族企业 (jiāzú qǐyè) is a family business , often meaning a business controlled across generations by a single clan. The word implies shared ancestry, shared surname, and historically shared clan property and ancestral hall (祠堂 cítáng).
嘉峪关	Jiā yù guān	Jiayu Pass — western terminus of the Ming wall
甲骨文	jiǎ gǔ wén	oracle bone script — the earliest Chinese writing — 甲 jiǎ (shell / armor) + 骨 gǔ (bone) + 文 wén (writing). Writing incised on turtle plastrons (腹甲 fùjiǎ) and ox scapulae (肩胛骨 jiānjiǎgǔ) used for royal divination during the Late Shang period. Over 4,000 distinct graphs have been identified; roughly 2,000 are deciphered.
甲子	jiǎ zǐ	the 60-year cycle; first combination
假花	jiǎ huā	artificial flower
假借	jiǎ jiè	to make use of, borrow (literary)
假如	jiǎ rú	supposing; if; in case — 假 jiǎ (false; supposed) + 如 rú (suppose). A slightly more formal "if," at home with imagined or hypothetical cases: 假如我是你 "supposing I were you." Interchangeable with 如果 in most sentences.
坚定	jiān dìng	firm; resolute
间接	jiàn jiē	indirect
兼爱	jiān ài	universal, impartial love — 兼 jiān (jointly; all together; simultaneously) + 爱 ài (love; care). The central Mohist doctrine: love that makes no distinction by relationship, proximity, or social standing. Contrasted directly with the Confucian 爱有差等 ài yǒu chā děng (love is graded by degree of relationship).
减少	jiǎn shǎo	to reduce; to decrease; to diminish — 减 jiǎn (to subtract, to reduce) + 少 (few, less). To make less: to reduce a quantity, frequency, or intensity. 减少污染 (reduce pollution); 减少开支 (cut expenditure); 人数减少了 (the number of people decreased). The standard verb for any reduction in quantity.
减字谱	jiǎn zì pǔ	abbreviated-character tablature — the guqin's notational system — 减 jiǎn (reduce; abbreviate) + 字 zì (character) + 谱 pǔ (notation; score). Developed in the Tang dynasty, used continuously since. Each composite character encodes string number, finger, position, and technique in a single glyph. Over 3,000 pieces survive in this notation.
碱水面	jiǎn shuǐ miàn	alkaline noodles; lye-water noodles
见面	jiàn miàn	to meet; to see one another (in person) — 见 jiàn (to see) + 面 miàn (face). Literally "to see faces," the everyday verb for meeting someone in person. It is a separable verb: 见了一面 "met once," 见过面 "have met before."
见外	jiàn wài	to treat as an outsider
建国	jiàng uó	to found a nation; nation-building — 建 jiàn (to build, to establish) + 国 (country, state). The founding of a state. 建国以来 (since the founding of the country) is a common phrase that, depending on speaker, refers to 1912 (Republic) or 1949 (People's Republic). 国庆节 (National Day, October 1) marks the 1949 建国.
健全	jiàn quán	sound; sound and whole
江北	jiāng běi	North of the Yangtze — North of the Yangtze. In local usage, particularly in Nanjing and Shanghai, this refers to the area immediately north of the river, considered rougher and less cosmopolitan than Jiangnan. A mild regional insult in some contexts.
江湖恩怨	jiāng hú ēn yuàn	the debts and vendettas of the martial world; the cycle of obligation and revenge — 江湖 + 恩 ēn (grace; debt of kindness) + 怨 yuàn (grievance; resentment). The engine of wuxia narratives: every favor creates an obligation, every wrong creates a grievance. The 江湖 keeps score across generations , a debt unpaid by the father falls to the son.
江湖人	jiāng hú rén	a person of the rivers-and-lakes world; someone who lives by the code — The person defined by their membership in the 江湖 , not just someone who passes through it, but someone whose identity is constituted by it. 江湖人 abide by a parallel moral code: loyalty to sworn brothers (义气 yìqi), repayment of debts and favors, the obligation to right wrongs.
江南	jiāng nán	south of the Yangtze; the culturally and economically rich lower Yangtze region — 江 jiāng (the Yangtze) + 南 nán (south). The region south of the Yangtze , centred on present-day Jiangsu, Zhejiang, and Shanghai , is the most romanticized landscape in classical Chinese poetry. 江南 means silk, canals, spring rain, white walls, and scholar gardens.
将来	jiāng lái	the future; in the future; what is coming — 将 jiāng (about to; will; the near future) + 来 lái (coming; arriving). What is about to arrive, the future approaching. 将来 refers to the general future (not an immediate event). Slightly more formal than 以后 yǐhòu. Contrast: 未来 wèilái (the future, lit.
讲文明	jiǎng wén míng	to be civilized; to behave with proper manners — The everyday descendant of the classical sense, deployed across public-conduct campaigns. Bus stops, parks, and tourist sites carry signs urging citizens to 讲文明 (be civilized) and 讲礼貌 (be polite).
交错	jiāo cuò	to interlace, crisscross, intersect — 交 jiāo (to cross) + 错 cuò (crosswise). The original "crossing" sense, with no fault implied: lines, paths, or branches weaving across one another. 纵横交错 describes a network running every which way.
交换	jiāo huàn	to exchange, swap — 交 (mutual) + 换 (swap). A two-way exchange where both sides hand something over: gifts, contact details, opinions, prisoners. More formal and reciprocal than a plain 换.
交领右衽	jiāo lǐng yòu rèn	cross-collar wrapping right-over-left — the defining feature of hànfú
交朋友	jiāo péng you	to make friends — A verb-object phrase: 交 jiāo (to associate with, to form bonds) + 朋友. To make a single friend is 交个朋友 (with 个 splitting the phrase). The same 交 appears in 交流 (to exchange, communicate) and 社交 (social interaction), all built on the sense of mutual exchange.
交通	jiāo tōng	traffic; transport; transportation — 交 jiāo (to cross; to exchange; mutual) + 通 (to go through; to connect). The crossing-and-passing of people and vehicles: the whole system of movement through a place. 交通 covers traffic in the everyday sense (交通堵塞, a traffic jam) and transport as a category (交通工具, means of transport).
交通灯	jiāo tōng dēng	traffic light — 交通 (jiāotōng, traffic) + 灯 (light): the three-color signal at an intersection. Also called 红绿灯 (hónglǜdēng), literally the red-green light.
浇头	jiāo tóu	noodle topping; the garnish that makes the bowl
焦点	jiāo diǎn	focus; focal point
饺子	jiǎo zi	dumplings — the northern New Year food — The quintessential northern Chinese New Year food, eaten at midnight on New Year's Eve in many northern households. The shape resembles the 元宝 yuánbǎo (ancient sycee gold ingots), making them symbols of wealth.
饺子皮	jiǎo zi pí	Dumpling wrapper, literally "dumpling skin — Dumpling wrapper, literally "dumpling skin." Sold pre-made in supermarkets, but making them by hand is considered superior.
较真	jiào zhēn	to be a stickler; to take something too seriously — 较 jiào (to compare; to dispute) + 真. To insist on getting the real, exact, literal version of something , usually said with mild disapproval. The cousin of 认真 with the dial turned past serviceable into pedantic.
教材	jiào cái	teaching material
教练	jiào liàn	coach, instructor — 教 (to teach) + 练 (to drill). The one who teaches by drilling: a sports coach, a driving instructor, a fitness trainer.
教师	jiào shī	teacher (formal); educator — 教 jiào (to teach) + 师 shī (master; expert). The formal word for a teacher as a profession, common in writing and titles. In direct address the warmer 老师 lǎoshī is used instead.
教室	jiào shì	classroom — 教 jiào (to teach) + 室 shì (room). The room where teaching happens. Watch the tone of 室 (shì), and do not confuse it with 教师 jiàoshī (teacher); the two words differ only in the second syllable.
教授	jiào shòu	professor
教堂	jiào táng	church (the building) — 教 jiào (religion) + 堂 táng (hall). The hall of worship, a church building. The community of believers, by contrast, is 教会 jiàohuì.
教训	jiào xun	a lesson (learned the hard way); to reprimand — 教 jiào (to teach) + 训 xùn (to admonish). As a noun, the lesson drawn from a mistake or setback: 吸取教训 "to learn the lesson." As a verb, to scold or take someone to task.
教育	jiào yù	education; to educate — 教 jiào (to teach) + 育 yù (to raise; to nurture). The whole enterprise of teaching and bringing up, as both a noun and a verb: 高等教育 "higher education," 教育孩子 "to bring up a child." This is the formal jiào reading.
接班	jiē bān	to take over a shift
