The first 了 marks the initial arrival (completed action of coming); the second 了 marks the current relevance , she is still here now. Without the second 了, the sentence implies the visit is concluded.
她们	tā men	they (all-female group) — 她 + 们 (men, plural marker). Used only when every member of the group is known to be female. If the group is mixed-gender or the gender is unknown, the default is 他们 (tāmen) , the male-radical form is the unmarked plural.
她是今天早上走的	tā shì jīn tiān zǎo shang zǒu de	she left this morning (FOCUS: this morning, not yesterday) — More precise time focus , this morning, as opposed to last night or yesterday. The presupposition is clear: she has already left (both speakers know this). The new information is the precise time of departure. Commonly used in conversations about arrivals, departures, and events.
她字	tā zì	"the 她 character" — shorthand for the gendered-pronoun debate — A meta-term in linguistic and feminist writing. 她字之争 ("the dispute over 她") refers to the recurring debate over whether to use 她, abolish it, or replace it with a non-marked alternative.
台词	tái cí	an actor lines; stage dialogue; script lines — 台 tái (stage) + 词 cí (words). The lines a performer speaks in a play, film, or show. 背台词 bèi táicí is to learn one lines by heart.
台灯	tái dēng	desk lamp, table lamp — 台 (tái, platform, stand) + 灯 (lamp): a lamp that sits on a desk or table, the small pool of light you read and study by.
台海	tái hǎi	The Taiwan Strait — The Taiwan Strait: the body of water between Taiwan and the Chinese mainland. Used as shorthand for the cross-strait political situation: 台海关系 (cross-strait relations), 台海局势 (Taiwan Strait situation).
抬头	tái tóu	to raise the head; to look up — 抬 tái (to lift) + 头 tóu (head). To lift the head and look up, literally or in the sense of holding one's head high after hardship. Its opposite is 低头 dītóu, to lower the head.
太后	tài hòu	empress dowager; the emperor's mother — 太 tài (grand; senior; beyond; supreme) + 后 (empress). The emperor's mother or grandmother who has survived her husband's reign.
太极	tài jí	the Supreme Ultimate; Taiji (the source of yin and yang) — 太 tài (supreme) + 极 jí (the pole; the ultimate). In the Yijing tradition, the Supreme Ultimate from which 阴 yīn and 阳 yáng divide. The familiar swirl symbol is the 太极图. The slow martial art is 太极拳 tàijíquán.
太极拳	tài jí quán	Tai Chi Chuan — the soft internal martial art based on yin-yang principles — 太极 tàijí (the Supreme Ultimate , the cosmological concept) + 拳 quán (fist; boxing style). The soft, circular martial art based on the principles of 阴阳: yielding overcomes force, softness overcomes hardness, stillness overcomes motion. A paradigm of 内功 (internal cultivation).
太监	tài jiān	eunuch official
太了	tài le	too… (excessive)
太平	tài píng	great peace — the classical political ideal
太平天国	Tài píng Tiāng uó	Taiping Heavenly Kingdom
太太	tài tai	Mrs.; madam; wife (polite) — The doubled 太, the senior lady of the house. Used as a title after a surname (王太太 "Mrs. Wang") and as a polite word for one's own or another's wife. The 太 marks seniority and respect.
太学	Tài xu é	Imperial Academy
太阳	tài yáng	the sun — 太 tài (supreme; ultimate; the greatest) + 阳 yáng (yang force; light). The Supreme Yang , the ultimate source of yang energy in the cosmos. In the 五行 framework, the sun is the source of Fire phase energy. 太阳能 (solar energy) uses exactly this logic.
坦白	tǎn bái	to confess; come clean
汤面	tāng miàn	noodles in soup; noodle soup
堂哥	táng gē	older male cousin (paternal)
逃跑	táo pǎo	to flee; to escape; to run away — 逃 (táo, to flee) + 跑. The standard verb for running away from a threat, a captor, or an obligation. Used for fugitives, escaped animals, soldiers fleeing a battlefield, and (figuratively) people who avoid responsibility.
讨论	tǎo lùn	to discuss; to talk over — 讨 tǎo (to seek out, to ask for) + 论. The everyday word for discussing a matter: a group talking something through to reach understanding or a decision. Cooperative by default, two or more people pooling views rather than fighting over them.
讨论会	tǎo lùn huì	a discussion forum; a symposium
套房	tào fáng	suite, self-contained flat
套路	tào lù	form; sequence; a fixed pattern (also: routine, formula) — A choreographed sequence of martial techniques practiced solo, encoding combat principles. Also used colloquially for any formulaic routine or pattern: 走套路 "going through the motions" · 那只是套路 "that's just standard formula."
特别是	tè bié shì	"Especially" or "particularly — "Especially" or "particularly." Singles out the most important case within a broader claim. 我喜欢中国菜, 特别是川菜 ("I like Chinese food, especially Sichuan food"). Direct, useful, common.
特意	tè yì	specially; on purpose (warm or neutral)
特长	tè cháng	special skill; particular strength; forte — 特 tè (special; particular) + 长 cháng (strength; advantage, the area where one "goes long"). 你有什么特长？ "What are your special skills?" is the standard job interview form. 特长生 = a student admitted on the basis of a special talent (music, sports, art) rather than exam scores alone.
提高	tí gāo	to raise; to improve; to enhance — 提 tí (to lift, to raise; to bring up) + 高 (high). To raise the level of something: 提高效率 (improve efficiency); 提高水平 (raise the standard); 提高工资 (increase wages). The standard verb for improvement in formal and professional contexts.
提前	tí qián	to move up in time; to do in advance; ahead of schedule — 提 tí (to lift; to bring forward; to raise up) + 前 qián (front; before). Literally "to lift forward": to move a scheduled event earlier than planned, to do something in advance. The opposite is 推迟 tuīchí (to postpone, to push back).
提问	tí wèn	to ask a question; to raise a question — 提 tí (to raise; to bring up) + 问 wèn (to ask). To put a question forward, typically in a class or meeting. 老师提问 "the teacher asks a question," often of a student called on to answer.
题款	tí kuǎn	inscription on a painting
题诗	tí shī	to inscribe a poem (on a painting, wall, or scroll) — 题 tí (to inscribe) + 诗 shī (poem). To write a poem directly onto a surface, a classic gesture in Chinese painting where verse and image share one scroll.
题字	tí zì	to inscribe characters; an inscription — 题 tí (to inscribe) + 字 zì (characters). To brush words onto a painting, plaque, or wall, or the inscription itself. Often done by a respected figure as a mark of honor.
体会	tǐ huì	to grasp through experience; the understanding so gained — 体 tǐ (body) + 会 huì (to understand). To come to understand a thing by living it, and the insight that results. 深刻的体会 "a deep understanding (won from experience)."
体积	tǐ jī	volume; bulk
体力	tǐ lì	physical stamina; bodily strength; endurance — 体 tǐ (body) + 力 lì (strength). The body's stamina, the strength that sustains physical effort over time. Often paired against 脑力 nǎolì (mental effort): 体力劳动 "physical labor."
体面	tǐ miàn	decent; respectable; dignified
体贴	tǐ tiē	considerate; thoughtful; attentive to others' needs — 体 tǐ (to feel in the body) + 贴 tiē (to stick close). To feel another's situation closely and act with care: 体贴的丈夫 "a considerate husband." It is warmth shown through attention to small needs.
体系	tǐ xì	framework; structured system (of ideas or institutions) — 体 tǐ (body; structure) + 系 xì (connection). An organized framework, usually of something abstract: 理论体系 "a theoretical framework," 教育体系 "the education system." More architectural and conceptual than the everyday 系统.
体验	tǐ yàn	to experience; to live through; an experience — 体 tǐ (body) + 验 yàn (to test, to verify). To know something by passing through it in person: 体验生活 "to experience real life." As a noun, the experience itself: 难忘的体验 "an unforgettable experience."
体用	tǐ yòng	substance and function; essence and manifestation — 体 tǐ (body; substance; the underlying essence) + 用 yòng (function; use; the outward expression). A compound used in philosophy, aesthetics, Buddhist thought, and political theory to describe the relationship between a thing's nature and its operation in the world.
体育	tǐ yù	physical education; sports — 体 tǐ (body) + 育 yù (to nurture, to educate). The cultivation of the body: school PE, and sport in general. 体育馆 tǐyùguǎn is a gymnasium; 体育课 is a PE class.
体育馆	tǐ yù guǎn	gymnasium, indoor arena — 体育 tǐyù (physical education, sport) + 馆. The roofed sports hall, as opposed to the open-air 体育场 (chǎng, a field/stadium). The 馆 marks the covered, indoor venue.
体质	tǐ zhì	Constitution — Constitution: one's inherent physical and energetic type, which determines predispositions to certain diseases and guides appropriate diet, lifestyle, and treatment choices. Nine constitutional types are defined in modern TCM standardization.
体重	tǐ zhòng	body weight — 体 tǐ (body) + 重 zhòng (weight). How much a person weighs: 量体重 "to weigh oneself." The weight that the body itself carries.
替换	tì huàn	to replace, substitute — 替 (in place of) + 换 (swap). To take one thing out and put a stand-in where it was: a worn part, a player, a placeholder word. The replacement fills the same slot the original left.
天才	tiān cái	genius; heaven-bestowed talent — 天 tiān (heaven) + 才 cái (talent; ability). Heaven-bestowed ability , the kind of capacity that seems to have come from beyond ordinary human effort. The etymology encodes a theory of talent: genius is a gift, not a product.
天大	tiān dà	sky-high; enormous (informal)
天地间	tiān dì jiān	between heaven and earth; in this world — 间 jiān (between; the space between two things). 天地间 names the space of human existence. A frequent location phrase in classical and literary prose.
天地人	tiān dì rén	Heaven, Earth, Humanity — the Three Powers (三才 sāncái) — The three constitutive powers of the cosmos in classical Chinese thought. Heaven (天) provides the patterns and conditions from above; Earth (地) provides the material ground and resources from below; Humanity (人) stands between them, participating in both and responsible for the mediation.
天赋	tiān fù	natural gift; innate ability; heaven's endowment — 天 tiān (heaven) + 赋 fù (to endow; to bestow; to compose, originally the government's levy on subjects, then poetic composition). Heaven's formal bestowal. 天赋 is slightly more formal and literary than 天才, referring to specific aptitudes rather than a person of exceptional overall genius.
天干	tiāng ān	Heavenly Stems
天干地支	tiāng ān dì zhī	heavenly stems and earthly branches; the traditional Chinese calendrical system — The 60-year calendrical cycle built on the same yang/yin cosmology that underlies 乾坤. 天干 tiāngān (heavenly stems): ten yang elements cycling through the years (甲 jiǎ, 乙 yǐ, 丙 bǐng, 丁 dīng, etc.).
天国	tiāng uó	Kingdom of Heaven (Christian usage) — 天 tiān (heaven) + 国 guó (kingdom; nation). The standard Chinese translation of "Kingdom of Heaven" in Christian scripture.
天后宫	Tiān hòu Gōng	Tianhou Palace — her temples across the diaspora — After her elevation to 天后 (Heavenly Empress) by the Qing Emperor in 1683, Mazu's temples were formally renamed 天后宫 Tiānhòu Gōng (Tianhou Palace).
天界	tiān jiè	the celestial realm; heaven; the realm of gods and immortals — 天 (heaven; sky) + 界 (boundary; realm; world). The realm above 人间, populated by gods (神 shén), immortals (仙 xiān), and celestial officials. In folk-religion cosmology it mirrors the imperial bureaucracy below, with the Jade Emperor (玉皇大帝 Yù Huáng Dàdì) at its head.
天色	tiān sè	color of the sky; time of day as read from the light — 天 tiān (sky) + 色 sè (color; hue; appearance). The quality of sky-light as a way of telling the time or mood of the day: dawn's pale gray, midday's high white, dusk's orange bleed. A literary and slightly classical usage, common in classical fiction and poetry.
天山	Tiān shān	The Heavenly Mountains — The Heavenly Mountains: the mountain range dividing northern and southern Xinjiang, with peaks reaching 7,443 m. A UNESCO World Natural Heritage Site, the Tianshan is the water source for the oasis cities of the Tarim Basin and one of the great mountain ranges of Central Asia.
天生	tiān shēng	innate; born with; naturally endowed — 天 tiān (heaven) + 生 shēng (to be born; to live). "Born of heaven" = innate, not acquired. The counterpart to 后天 hòutiān (acquired; postnatal). In classical philosophy, the 先天/后天 dichotomy maps roughly onto nature/nurture, one of the most enduring debates in Chinese intellectual life.
天台宗	Tiān tái Zōng	Tiantai — the comprehensive synthesis school — Founded by Zhiyi 智顗 (538–597 CE) on Mount Tiantai 天台山 in Zhejiang. Tiantai's great achievement was doctrinal classification 判教 pānjìao: organizing all Buddhist scriptures into a hierarchical scheme of "five periods and eight teachings," with the Lotus Sutra 法华经 at the apex.
天堂	tiān táng	paradise; heaven as a place of bliss — 天 tiān (heaven) + 堂 táng (hall; grand hall; a place of gathering). The "heavenly hall," used in Chinese Buddhism for the Pure Land, and in general usage for any paradise-like place. Also commonly used hyperbolically: 美食天堂 (food paradise), 购物天堂 (shopping paradise), 自行车天堂 (cycling paradise).
天文	tiān wén	Astronomy — Astronomy: heaven's patterns. 天文数字 ("an astronomical figure") is the standard idiom for an unthinkably large sum, same metaphor as English.
天下第一	tiān xià dì yī	number one under heaven; the best in the world; the supreme superlative — The hyperbolic superlative: beyond which there is nothing greater. 天下 replaces "in the world," and 第一 means "first/number one." Used for scenic spots (桂林山水甲天下 , Guilin's scenery is finest under heaven), for self-proclaimed champions (自称天下第一 , to call oneself the best in the world), and in historical…
天意	tiān yì	the will of Heaven; what fate decrees
天真	tiān zhēn	naive; innocent; childlike; guileless — 天 tiān (heaven) + 真 zhēn (genuine; true; real). "Heaven-true" , the quality of someone whose genuine nature has not been shaped or distorted by social calculation. In children, this is charming: 天真烂漫 (innocent and carefree , the enchanting naivety of childhood).
天志	tiān zhì	the will of Heaven — Mohist moral authority — Unlike Confucian ethics, which grounds moral authority in tradition, the sage-kings, and the cultivation of 仁 rén (humaneness), Mozi appealed to 天 tiān (Heaven) as a purposive moral agent that actively wills universal love and punishes those who violate it.
天子	tiān zǐ	Son of Heaven — the Emperor — 天 tiān (heaven) + 子 zǐ (son; child). The Chinese emperor's formal title: he is heaven's son, the intermediary between the cosmic order and humanity, not a god himself but heaven's appointed representative on earth. The title dates to the Zhou dynasty and was used through the Qing (ended 1912).
铁路	tiě lù	railway
听从	tīng cóng	to comply with; to follow (advice, instructions) — 听 (to listen, to heed) + 从 cóng (to follow; to comply). To hear and act accordingly , to accept and follow guidance, instructions, or authority. Used for following medical advice (听从医嘱 tīngcóng yīzhǔ), following a superior's direction, or complying with a decision.
听到	tīng dào	to hear; to catch (a sound) — 听 tīng (to listen) + 到 dào (arrive). Listening that arrived: the sound reached the ear and registered. 听到 is not an attempt to hear but a confirmed result. Compare 听 alone (the action of listening) with 听到 (the listening that succeeded).
听懂	tīng dǒng	to understand what one hears; to follow spoken language — 听 (to listen) + 懂 dǒng (to understand). The resultative complement 懂 marks successful comprehension , listening that reached understanding. Constant in language learning contexts: 你听懂了吗？ (Did you understand what you heard?) contrasts with 你听到了吗？ (Did you hear it? — did the sound reach you?).
听话	tīng huà	obedient; to do as one is told — 听 (to listen) + 话 huà (words; speech). Obedient in the sense of listening to what is said and acting accordingly. The standard word for a well-behaved child: 这孩子很听话 (this child is very obedient).
听见	tīng jiàn	to hear
听起来	tīng qǐ lái	sounds like
听说	tīng shuō	to have heard (that); reportedly; I've heard that — 听 (to hear) + 说 shuō (to say; to speak). A hedging marker for secondhand information: "I've heard it said that…" Placed at the start of a clause to flag that the speaker did not witness the information directly. 听说他去北京了 (I heard he went to Beijing).
听音乐	tīng yīn yu è	to listen to music — 听 (to listen) + 音乐 yīnyuè (music , 音 sound/tone + 乐 joy/music). The most common collocation for 听 in daily life. 听歌 tīng gē (to listen to songs) is the slightly more casual form.
听着！	tīng zhe !	Listen! / Listen up! (and stay listening) — The sustained-attention imperative with 着. 听 alone is a simple command to listen; 听着 adds the sense of "maintain that listening state." Common before announcements, reprimands, or instructions that require focused, continuous attention.
停电	tíng diàn	power outage
通常	tōng cháng	usually; ordinarily; generally; as a rule — 通 (general; holding throughout) + 常 cháng (constant; usual; frequent). The case that holds across most instances: usually, as the general rule. 通常 sets up the ordinary expectation against which exceptions are measured: 我通常七点起床 (I usually get up at seven).
通车	tōng chē	to be open to traffic; to begin service (of a road or line) — 通 (to open; to be through) + 车 chē (vehicle). To be through to vehicles: the moment a new road, bridge, railway, or subway line opens and traffic can run along it. A staple of infrastructure news, where the opening of a line is itself an event: 正式通车 (officially opened to traffic).
通过	tōng guò	to pass through; to get past; by means of; via — 通 (to go through) + 过 guò (to cross; to pass). The everyday verb for getting through something: passing through a gate, passing an exam, a motion passing a vote.
通信	tōng xìn	to correspond; to communicate; telecommunications — 通 (to connect; to convey) + 信 xìn (a letter; a message; trust). To get messages through to one another. Historically it meant to exchange letters: 我们通了多年信 (we corresponded for many years).
通用	tōng yòng	general-purpose; in common use; interchangeable — 通 (general; running throughout) + 用 yòng (to use). Usable throughout, across cases: not specialized to one situation but serving generally. 通用 describes something accepted everywhere or applicable across the board: 全国通用 (valid nationwide), 通用规则 (a general rule that applies in all cases).
通知	tōng zhī	to notify; to inform; a notice; a notification — 通 (to get through; to convey) + 知 zhī (to know; to make known). To make something known by getting word through: the standard word for an official notification and the act of giving it. Works as a verb (请通知大家, please notify everyone) and a noun (一份通知, a notice).
同伴	tóng bàn	companion; fellow
同胞	tóng bāo	compatriot; fellow citizen
同时	tóng shí	at the same time; meanwhile; moreover — 同 tóng (same) + 时 shí (time). The same moment: 同时进行 "to proceed simultaneously." As a connector it also means "at the same time, moreover," adding a further point.
同事	tóng shì	colleague; co-worker — 同 tóng (same) + 事 shì (work; affair). One who shares the same work, a colleague. Built on the same pattern as 同学: the shared station named by what is held in common.
同学	tóng xu é	classmate; fellow student; (as address) student — 同 tóng (same) + 学 xué (study). One who studies alongside you. A teacher also uses 同学 to address a pupil: 这位同学 "this student here." 老同学 means an old schoolmate.
同样	tóng yàng	same; alike; likewise — 同 tóng (same) + 样 yàng (kind). Of the same kind, used before a noun (同样的方法 "the same method") or as a sentence adverb meaning "in the same way, likewise."
同意	tóng yì	to agree; to consent; to approve — 同 tóng (same) + 意 yì (idea; intention). To hold the same idea, to give one's assent: 我同意你的看法 "I agree with your view." The opposite is 反对 fǎnduì, to oppose.
同志	tóng zhì	comrade; (colloquial) a gay person — 同 tóng (same) + 志 zhì (aspiration; will). One of the same aspiration. The standard revolutionary address for "comrade," and in contemporary informal use a self-chosen word for gay people.
铜人	tóng rén	Bronze man — Bronze man: the life-sized bronze figure commissioned in 1026 CE with channel lines and point locations engraved on the surface and a hollow interior. Filled with water and wax for examination purposes, it was the world's first standardized anatomical teaching model for acupuncture instruction.
童话	tóng huà	fairy tale; children's story — 童 tóng (child) + 话 huà (story). A tale told for children. Its kin 神话 shénhuà (god-story) means a myth, and 神话 and 童话 together cover the world of fabulous stories.
童年	tóng nián	childhood
统一	tǒng yī	unification; to unify; uniform — 统 tǒng (to govern; to bind together) + 一. The political constant of Chinese civilization: 天下统一 (unification of all under heaven). As verb: 统一思想 (unify thinking), 统一标准 (standardize). As adjective: 统一规格 (uniform specifications).
痛快	tòng kuài	frank and direct; thoroughly enjoyable — 痛 tòng (pain; with full intensity) + 快 kuài (free-flowing; pleasurable). Two distinct but related uses. As a personality or speech descriptor: so direct and honest it cuts , blunt, refreshingly frank. As an experience descriptor: so wholly satisfying that nothing was held back.
头发	tóu fa	hair (on the head) — 头 tóu (head) + 发 fà (hair). The hair on a person's head, as a mass. Note that 发 here is the fà / 髮 reading; in casual speech the syllable often softens toward neutral tone. Distinct from 毛 máo, the word for body hair and fur.
头七	tóu qī	the first seven-day memorial after death — 头 tóu (head; first) + 七 qī (seven). Traditional Chinese mourning involves rituals at seven-day intervals for 49 days , the period the soul is believed to linger. 头七 (day 7) is the most important: the family burns paper offerings and sets a place for the deceased at the table.
头疼，不能去	tóu téng , bù néng qù	headache, can't go — circumstance prevents ability — 能 covers physical capacity (your body can or cannot do something) and circumstantial ability (the situation allows or does not allow).
投票	tóu piào	to vote: to cast a ballot — 投 tóu (to throw; to cast; to drop in) + 票 piào (slip; ballot). To vote, literally to throw a slip into the box. The image is exact: a ballot is a slip of paper, and casting it is dropping it into the urn.
突然	tū rán	sudden; abrupt; suddenly — 突 tū (to dash out, abrupt) + 然 rán (-ly). Unlike 忽然, 突然 can also be a full adjective: 很突然 "very sudden," 突然的变化 "an abrupt change." So it covers both "suddenly" and "abrupt."
团队	tuán duì	team (working unit) — 团 (a tight cluster, a ball of people) + 队 (organized group). The modern office and startup word for a project team bound by a shared task.
团结	tuán jié	to unite; unity; solidarity — 团 tuán (round; group) + 结 jié (to bind). To draw a group tightly together into one body: 团结一致 "united as one." Used of teams, communities, and nations holding together against difficulty.
团体	tuán tǐ	organization; group
推手	tuī shǒu	pushing hands — the partner practice of Tai Chi
退火	tuì huǒ	annealing — Slow controlled cooling that removes internal stress. Poor annealing is a cause of bottles cracking under temperature changes later. 应力 (yìnglì) is stress; a 应力仪 (stress viewer) shows it as coloured bands.
退热	tuì rè	to bring down a fever — 退 (tuì, to recede) plus 热 (heat, fever): to make the fever fall. The word on a fever-reducing medicine box and what a worried parent waits for through the night.
脱离	tuō lí	to break away from, to detach from — 脱 (tuō, to shed, to slip off) plus 离 (to part). To break free of a tie, a danger, or a group. Stronger than 离开: it implies cutting a connection, not just walking out.
驼队	tuó duì	camel train
袜子	wà zi	socks
外表	wài biǎo	outward appearance: the way someone or something looks from outside — 外 wài (outer; outside) + 表 biǎo (surface). The outward appearance of a person or thing, especially how they look to others. Where 表面 leans toward physical surfaces and abstract superficiality, 外表 is most natural for the looks of people and objects, the impression they give at first sight.
外部	wài bù	external; the outside
外丹	wài dān	outer alchemy — elixirs of immortality — 外 wài (outer) + 丹 dān (cinnabar). The earlier tradition: physically preparing elixirs in a laboratory by heating and transforming minerals (especially cinnabar, lead, gold, and sulfur) to produce an immortality drug.
外发	wài fā	to send out (to a subcontractor) — The question the tour exists to answer. A factory that sends out a step is not necessarily hiding anything, but you need to know, because quality on that step is out of its direct control.
外国	wài guó	foreign country; abroad — 外 wài (outside) + 国 guó (country). A country other than one''s own. A person from one is 外国人 wàiguórén (foreigner); the things of one are 外国 X, as in 外国文化 "foreign culture."
外交	wài jiāo	diplomacy
外卖	wài mài	takeout; food delivery — 外 wài (out) + 卖 mài (to sell). Food sold to be taken away rather than eaten in. In modern usage it means app-based delivery: 点外卖 "to order takeout," 外卖员 "a delivery rider."
外面	wài miàn	outside; the outside; outdoors — 外 wài (outside) + 面 miàn (side; surface). The everyday word for the area beyond an enclosed space, or the outer surface of a thing. Its pair is 里面 lǐmiàn (inside).
外婆	wài pó	maternal grandmother (mother''s mother) — 外 wài (outside the surname) + 婆 pó (old woman). One''s mother''s mother, marked 外 because she belongs to the maternal line. Its pair is 外公 wàigōng (maternal grandfather); the father''s parents are 奶奶 and 爷爷.
外滩	Wài tān	The Bund , the waterfront promenade along the Huangpu River, lined with the banking and trading buildings of the concession era. The name means literally "outer mudflat." Now a heritage district and one of the most photographed urban frontages in China.
外语	wài yǔ	foreign language — 外 wài (foreign) + 语 yǔ (language). A language other than one''s own. 学外语 "to study a foreign language," 外语学院 "a foreign-languages institute."
完成	wán chéng	to complete; to accomplish; to finish — 完 wán (whole; to finish) + 成 chéng (to bring to completion). To carry a task all the way to done: 完成任务 "to complete a task," 完成作业 "to finish the homework." The everyday word for getting something fully finished.
完好	wán hǎo	intact; in good condition
完美	wán měi	perfect; flawless
完全	wán quán	completely; entirely; fully — 完 wán (finished; whole) + 全 quán (complete). The standard adverb for "completely," to the full degree with nothing left over: 完全同意 "completely agree," 完全没问题 "no problem at all." Negated, it gives "not at all": 完全不对.
完全同意	wán quán tóng yì	"Completely agree — "Completely agree." Strong endorsement. Used when you want to back the speaker without reservation. The 完全 ("completely") makes it emphatic; reserve for moments when full agreement is what you actually mean.
完整	wán zhěng	complete; whole; intact
玩笑	wán xiào	a joke; a prank; banter — 玩 wán (to play) + 笑 xiào (laugh). Play-laughter: a joke made between people who are at ease together. 开玩笑 kāi wánxiào (to crack a joke, to be kidding) is one of the highest-frequency phrases in spoken Mandarin.
玩笑话	wán xiào huà	words said in jest
万里	wàn lǐ	ten thousand li; immense distance; vast — 万 (ten thousand) + 里 lǐ (Chinese unit of distance, roughly 500m). Ten thousand li is nominally 5,000 km, but the compound functions as a hyperbole for any vast, daunting distance. 万里长城 (Wànlǐ Chángchéng, "the ten-thousand-li Great Wall") is the most famous usage.
万事如意	wàn shì rú yì	may all ten thousand things go as you wish; all the best — 万 (myriad) + 事 shì (matters, things) + 如意 rúyì (as one wishes). The standard all-occasion blessing, appearing on red envelopes, greeting cards, New Year couplets, and toasts.
万岁	wàn suì	ten thousand years; long live (acclamation) — 万 (ten thousand) + 岁 suì (years of age; year). The imperial acclamation: 皇帝万岁 (huángdì wàn suì), "long live the Emperor." Reserved exclusively for the emperor in traditional usage; to use it for anyone else was a capital offense.
万物	wàn wù	all things; the myriad phenomena; everything that exists — 万 (myriad) + 物 wù (thing, object, phenomenon). The standard classical and literary expression for "all of existence." In Daoist thought, the ten-thousand things emerge from the interplay of yin and yang, and the Dao underlies them all.
万象	wàn xiàng	all phenomena; the ten thousand forms
万一	wàn yī	just in case; in the unlikely event that; one in ten thousand — 万 (ten thousand) + 一 (one). Literally, one part in ten thousand , the probability so small it barely registers. In usage: a concession to contingency.
网红	wǎng hóng	internet celebrity
网站	wǎng zhàn	website — 网 (wǎng, net) + 站 (station): a website, a fixed point on the internet. The everyday word for any site you visit, from a shop to a search engine.
往年	wǎng nián	former years
往生	wǎng shēng	going toward rebirth — the Pure Land term for death as aspiration — 往 wǎng (to go toward) + 生 shēng (life; rebirth). The Pure Land term for a practitioner's death, framed not as loss but as aspiration fulfilled.
忘记	wàng jì	to forget
忘我	wàng wǒ	selfless absorption; to forget oneself
微小	wēi xiǎo	tiny; minuscule; microscopic — 微 wēi (minute; subtle; trace) + 小 xiǎo (small). Doubles the smallness: 微 already indicates near-invisibility (as in 微生物 microorganism, 微波 microwave), and 小 compounds that. Used for things at the scale of cells, particles, or imperceptible contributions. A higher register than 很小.
微笑	wēi xiào	to smile; a smile — 微 wēi (faint; slight; minute) + 笑 xiào (laugh/smile). A small, soundless laugh: the smile. The word that names the warm, closed-lipped expression, distinct from the open-mouthed 大笑.
违法	wéi fǎ	to break the law; to act illegally — 违 wéi (to violate; to go against) + 法 fǎ (law). To step over the line the law draws: 违法行为 "an unlawful act." The mirror image of 合法, the foot landing outside the level mark.
唯一	wéi yī	the only one; sole; unique — 唯 wéi (only; alone) + 一. Used before nouns: 唯一的选择 (the only choice), 唯一的儿子 (only son). Stronger than 只有 zhǐyǒu in its emphasis on singularity , the uniqueness is total, not merely circumstantial. In formal or literary contexts: 唯一真神 (the one true God, in religious translation).
维系	wéi xì	to hold together; to maintain (a bond) — 维 wéi (to tie; to hold) + 系 xì (bind). To keep a relationship or order from coming apart: 维系感情 "to keep a bond alive," 维系和平 "to maintain peace." Two binding words doubled, the threads kept firmly in hand.
伟大	wěi dà	great; magnificent; lofty — 伟 wěi (towering, magnificent) + 大 dà. The highest register of greatness, used for great historical figures, great achievements, and (frequently in political contexts) the greatness of the Party or state. In everyday speech it can be slightly ironic if overused.
伪君子	wěi jūn zǐ	hypocrite; false gentleman — 伪 wěi (false, counterfeit) + 君子. A person who performs the outward markers of virtue without the inward character , the one who speaks of 义 while calculating 利. The word's force comes precisely from the prestige of what it negates.
卫生	wèi shēng	hygiene; health
卫生间	wèi shēng jiān	bathroom; toilet; restroom — 卫生 wèishēng (hygiene) + 间 jiān (room). The polite, standard word for a bathroom in homes and public buildings. A common synonym is 洗手间 xǐshǒujiān.
卫星	wèi xīng	satellite (natural or artificial) — 卫 (guard) + 星: the guard star that attends a planet. Covers the moon, the GPS constellation, and the dish antenna (卫星天线). The Great Leap Forward idiom 放卫星 ("launching a satellite") still means announcing an inflated achievement.
为此	wèi cǐ	for this reason; because of this; to this end — 为 wèi (for; on account of) + 此 cǐ (this; the aforementioned). A formal connective linking back to a preceding reason or context and introducing what was done in response. Stronger in register than 因此 yīncǐ (therefore), carrying a sense of specific, deliberate response to the stated situation.
为了	wèi le	in order to; for the sake of; for the purpose of — 为 wèi (for; toward) + 了 le (grammatical completion particle; here a suffix to 为). The standard purpose marker in modern Chinese. 为了 introduces the goal or beneficiary of an action and can appear at the start of a sentence or clause.
为人所知	wèi rén suǒ zhī	to be known by people; to become widely known
为所欲为	wèi suǒ yù wéi	to do whatever one pleases; to act without restraint
未来	wèi lái	the future (not yet arrived)
位于	wèi yú	to be located at; to be situated in — 位 wèi (position) + 于 yú (at). The standard verb for stating where a place sits, common in geography and description: 北京位于中国北部 "Beijing is located in the north of China."
温饱	wēn bǎo	warmth and food; subsistence — 温 (wēn, warm) + 饱 (fed): warm enough and fed enough, the baseline of a decent life. 温饱 names the threshold of basic livelihood in economic and political language, as in 解决温饱问题 (jiějué wēnbǎo wèntí, to solve the subsistence problem).
温和	wēn hé	gentle; mild; moderate
文笔	wén bǐ	writing style; literary skill — 文 wén (writing; letters) + 笔 bǐ (pen). The quality of someone's prose, the line their pen draws across a page: 文笔流畅 "fluent, flowing style." Praise or critique aimed at how a thing is written, not what it says.
文房	wén fáng	The scholar study, the room where the Four Treasures are kept and used. Literally "the room of writing." The aesthetic of the study, quiet, ordered, with good natural light, was itself a moral and aesthetic statement about the conditions necessary for serious intellectual and artistic work.
文件	wén jiàn	document; file; paperwork — 文 wén (writing; text) + 件 jiàn (a piece). A written document, an official paper, or a computer file. The word covers both physical paperwork and digital files: on a screen, 文件 is exactly the word for a file, and 文件夹 wénjiànjiā is a folder.
文人	wén rén	literati — the scholarly class who cultivated the guqin — 文 wén (culture; writing; civil) + 人 rén (person). The educated class in imperial China: scholars, poets, painters, officials who defined their identity through cultural cultivation rather than martial or commercial pursuits. The guqin was, more than any other object, the emblem of 文人 identity.
文人画	wén rén huà	literati painting; scholar painting — 文人 wénrén (literatus; cultivated scholar-official) + 画 huà (painting). The tradition of painting as personal cultivation and self-expression rather than as commissioned craft. Theorized by Su Shi 苏轼 and Mi Fu 米芾 in the Northern Song, dominant from the Yuan onward.
文殊	Wén shū	Mañjuśrī — bodhisattva of wisdom — Sanskrit Mañjuśrī ("Gentle Glory"). The bodhisattva of wisdom 智慧 zhìhuì, depicted holding a flaming sword (to cut through delusion) and riding a blue lion. Associated with Mount Wutai 五台山 in Shanxi, China's sacred mountain for wisdom, where Mañjuśrī was believed to reside and manifest.
文学	wén xu é	literature — 文 wén (writing; culture; pattern) + 学 xué (learning). The discipline of literature , both the creative output (novels, poetry, essays) and the scholarly study of it. 文学家 wénxuéjiā = writer, literary figure. 中国文学 = Chinese literature. 古典文学 = classical literature. 现代文学 = modern literature.
文章	wén zhāng	essay; article
文字	wén zì	written language; script; characters collectively — 文 wén (writing; culture; pattern; civilization) + 字 zì (character). Broader than 字 alone. 文字 covers the entire written tradition: individual characters, the system, the culture, and the civilization that writing makes possible.
稳定	wěn dìng	stable; steady; to stabilize — 稳 wěn (steady; firm) + 定 dìng (settled). Holding level without swinging, said of prices, jobs, moods, signals, and states. 稳定的工作 "a stable job," 局势稳定 "the situation is stable."
问候	wèn hòu	to send regards; to greet; to ask after someone — 问 wèn (to ask after) + 候 hòu (to wait on; to attend). To inquire warmly after another person, in speech or in writing: 致以亲切的问候 "to extend warm regards."
蜗牛	wō niú	snail — 蜗 (wō, the snail's shell, with the 虫 insect radical) + 牛. The little horns of a snail earned it the ox; the word doubles as a byword for anything slow.
我不太同意	wǒ bù tài tóng yì	"I don't quite agree — "I don't quite agree." The 不太 ("not too") softens a disagreement into something closer to "I'm not sure about that." Polite, leaves room for further discussion. Common in considered conversations where you want to push back without confrontation.
我的	wǒ de	my, mine
我见	wǒ jiàn	personal view; one's own opinion
我觉得	wǒ ju é de	"I feel" or "I think — "I feel" or "I think." The default opinion frame in spoken Chinese. Softer than 我认为 (wǒ rènwéi, "I hold the view that"), which is the more formal written-register equivalent. 我觉得 carries no special weight; it is the natural way to introduce any personal take.
我来帮你 · 他去买菜	wǒ lái bāng nǐ tā qù mǎi cài	I came to help you · he went to buy groceries — 来 and 去 can be followed directly by a verb of purpose: "come/go in order to do [V]." This is structurally simple: 来/去 + verb phrase. The motion establishes the direction; the following verb specifies the purpose of the trip. This is one of the most frequent patterns in everyday speech.
我们	wǒ men	we, us
我明白了	wǒ míng bai le	I understand now; I get it (I have entered a state of understanding) — State-change verbs like 明白 (understand), 知道 (know), 懂 (grasp), 累 (tired), 饿 (hungry), 高兴 (happy) pair powerfully with sentence-final 了. The 了 marks the entry into that state , the moment the change occurs. Before: didn't understand. After: understands.
我是在北京出生的	wǒ shì zài Běi jīng chū shēng de	I was born in Beijing (FOCUS: in Beijing, not elsewhere) — Place focus with 在 + location between 是 and the verb. The presupposition is that the speaker was born , the focus is the location. This is how Chinese speakers typically answer "Where were you born?" — using 是…的 to frame the location as the focused new information.
我要红的	wǒ yào hóng de	I want the red one — 的 stands alone as nominal — When the noun after 的 is already understood from context, it can be dropped , leaving 的 to function as a standalone noun phrase meaning "the one(s) that are X." This is the nominalizing function of 的, productive across all the adjective and clause patterns above.
我也这么觉得	wǒ yě zhè me ju é de	"I feel the same way" or "I think so too — "I feel the same way" or "I think so too." Personal endorsement that aligns your view with the speaker's. Warmer than a simple agreement; signals shared perspective.
我一说他就明白了	wǒ yī shuō tā jiù míng bai le	I said it once and he immediately understood — 一 + Verb + 就 is one of the most important patterns in the language: "as soon as / the moment I did X, immediately Y happened." The 就 marks the near-instant consequence , no delay, no difficulty.
我有不同的看法	wǒ yǒu bù tóng de kàn fǎ	"I see it differently" or "I have a different view — "I see it differently" or "I have a different view." The most direct of the gentle disagreement frames. Acknowledges the other view exists and explicitly marks your own as separate. Useful when you actually want to make the disagreement clear.
卧房	wò fáng	bedroom — 卧 (wò, to lie down) plus 房. The room for sleeping. In everyday speech 卧室 (wòshì) is now more common, but 卧房 survives and shows the same logic: the chamber where one lies down.
握手	wò shǒu	to shake hands — 握 wò (to grasp) + 手 shǒu (hand). To shake hands , the standard formal greeting gesture in modern Chinese business and official contexts. Traditional Chinese greeting was bowing or clasping one's own hands together (拱手 gǒngshǒu) rather than hand-to-hand contact.
五常	wǔ cháng	the Five Constants; the five cardinal virtues of Confucian ethics — 仁 rén · 义 yì · 礼 lǐ · 智 zhì · 信 xìn. The five virtues held to be universal moral constants across cultures and times.
五谷	wǔ gǔ	the five grains; staple crops — 五 wǔ + 谷 gǔ (grain). The five staple grains of ancient China, variously listed (commonly rice, two kinds of millet, wheat, and beans). The exact list shifted by region and era, but the number stayed fixed at five.
五官	wǔ guān	the five facial features; the five senses — 五 wǔ + 官 guān (organ; office). The five sense organs of the face: eyes, ears, nose, tongue, and (in the common reckoning) the mouth or skin.
五经	Wǔ Jīng	the Five Classics — the canonical texts of Confucian education — 五 wǔ (five) + 经 jīng (classic; canonical text; warp thread of a fabric). The five texts that defined literate education from the Han dynasty through the abolition of the imperial examination system in 1905: 诗经 (Classic of Poetry), 书经 (Classic of Documents), 易经 (Classic of Changes), 礼记 (Record of Rit…
五色	wǔ sè	the five colors — green-blue, red, yellow, white, black — 五 wǔ (five) + 色 sè (color, appearance). The five phase-colors: 青 qīng (green-blue, Wood), 红 hóng (red, Fire), 黄 huáng (yellow, Earth), 白 bái (white, Metal), 黑 hēi (black, Water). These are cosmological categories before they are aesthetic ones.
五四	Wǔ Sì	May Fourth (Movement) — context for 她's birth
五四运动	wǔ sì yùn dòng	May Fourth Movement — May Fourth Movement: the intellectual and cultural movement sparked by student protests on May 4, 1919, against the terms of the Treaty of Versailles. It catalyzed demands for science and democracy, rejection of Confucian tradition, and the development of a new vernacular literature.
五味	wǔ wèi	the five flavors — sour, bitter, sweet, pungent, salty — 五 wǔ (five) + 味 wèi (flavor, taste). Sour 酸 suān (Wood/Liver), bitter 苦 kǔ (Fire/Heart), sweet 甘 gān (Earth/Spleen), pungent/spicy 辛 xīn (Metal/Lung), salty 咸 xián (Water/Kidney). Each flavor enters and supports its corresponding organ system in moderate amounts; in excess, it taxes it.
五星红旗	wǔ xīng hóng qí	the Five-Starred Red Flag (flag of the PRC) — 五 wǔ + 星 xīng (star) + 红旗 hóngqí (red flag). The national flag of the People's Republic of China: one large star surrounded by four smaller ones on a red field.
五脏	wǔ zàng	the five yin organ systems — heart, liver, spleen, lung, kidney — 五 wǔ (five) + 脏 zàng (solid, yin, storing organ). In TCM, 五脏 refers to five functional systems, not five anatomical organs in the Western sense. Heart (心 xīn) , Fire; Liver (肝 gān) , Wood; Spleen (脾 pí) , Earth; Lung (肺 fèi) , Metal; Kidney (肾 shèn) , Water.
戊己庚辛	wù jǐ gēng xīn	Stems 5–8 (Earth &amp; Metal)
物理	wù lǐ	physics; the principles of matter — 物 wù (thing; matter) + 理 lǐ (principle). Literally "the principles of things," the modern name for physics. A clear case of 理 meaning the lawful pattern running through the physical world.
物体	wù tǐ	object; physical body — 物 wù (thing) + 体 tǐ (body). A physical object regarded as a body occupying space, the term used in physics and plain description alike: 不明飞行物 "an unidentified flying object."
物质	wù zhì	matter; substance; material — 物 wù (thing) + 质 zhì (substance). Matter in the scientific sense, and the material as opposed to the spiritual: 化学物质 "a chemical substance," 物质需求 "material needs."
物质文明	wù zhì wén míng	material civilization
夕阳	xī yáng	the setting sun; evening sun — 夕 xī (dusk; evening) + 阳 yáng (sun). The low sun of late day. By extension 夕阳 marks anything in graceful decline: 夕阳产业 "a sunset industry," the mirror of the 朝阳 sunrise one.
西安	xī ān	Xi'an, the nearby city
西餐	xī cān	Western food; Western cuisine — 西 (western) + 餐 (meal, cuisine). As opposed to 中餐 zhōngcān (Chinese food). In Chinese urban culture, 西餐厅 (Western restaurant) typically means a European-style restaurant with table settings, bread, and fork-and-knife service.
西藏	Xī zàng	Tibet
西方	xī fāng	the West; western direction; Pure Land (Buddhist) — 西 (west) + 方 (direction; region). The context determines which of the three senses is meant: cosmological/Buddhist (the Pure Land), historical-geographic (Western Regions), or modern geopolitical (the liberal West). When paired with 东方, it is almost always the modern cultural/civilizational sense.
西湖	Xī Hú	West Lake (Hangzhou) — 西 (west) plus 湖 (lake): the storied lake at Hangzhou, ringed by causeways and pagodas, the most painted and most quoted lake in Chinese poetry.
西施	Xī Shī	Xi Shi, one of the Four Beauties
西洋	Xī yáng	Western Ocean (Indian Ocean world)
西医	xī yī	Western medicine
西游记	Xī yóu jì	Journey to the West (16th-century novel) — 西 (west) + 游 (journey, travel) + 记 (record, account). The novel by Wú Chéng'ēn (吴承恩, c. 1500–1582) following the monk Xuánzàng, the Monkey King (孙悟空 Sūn Wùkōng), Pig (猪八戒 Zhū Bājiè), and Sandy (沙僧 Shā Sēng) on their journey to India to retrieve Buddhist scriptures.
西域	xī yù	the Western Regions
西周	Xī Zhōu	Western Zhou
习题	xí tí	practice exercises
洗脸	xǐ liǎn	to wash one's face — 洗 (to wash) + 脸 (face). The first ritual of the morning. A verb-object pair, so you can split it: 洗个脸 (xǐ ge liǎn), to give the face a quick wash.
喜爱	xǐ ài	to love; to be fond of; to favor — 喜 xǐ (to delight in) + 爱 ài (to love). A warmer, more literary cousin of 喜欢, often used of things admired or cherished: 深受喜爱 "deeply loved (by the public)," 喜爱的作家 "a favorite author."
喜好	xǐ hào	a liking; a preference; to be keen on — 喜 xǐ (to like) + 好 hào (to be fond of, 4th tone). Both a verb and a noun for a settled preference or taste: 个人喜好 "personal preferences," 喜好读书 "fond of reading." Note 好 here is hào, not hǎo.
喜钱	xǐ qián	happy money — cash gifts at joyous occasions
喜庆	xǐ qìng	Auspicious, festive — Auspicious, festive: the quality of being celebratory and lucky. Red is described as 喜庆 by default; white and black are explicitly not 喜庆 in gift or festive contexts.
喜丧	xǐ sāng	A "joyful funeral" — A "joyful funeral": the death of a person who lived to old age and left many descendants. Treated as cause for celebration alongside mourning, and marked with comparatively festive elements: the music may be more exuberant, the occasion more public.
喜事	xǐ shì	a happy occasion; a joyous event; (esp.) a wedding — 喜 xǐ (joy) + 事 shì (matter, affair). Any auspicious event, and by default a wedding. Its standing opposite is 丧事 sāngshì, a funeral, the unhappy event.
喜悦	xǐ yu è	joy; delight; gladness — 喜 xǐ (joy) + 悦 yuè (pleased). A literary, slightly elevated word for inner gladness, the quiet glow of a good thing: 丰收的喜悦 "the joy of a good harvest," 内心的喜悦 "the delight within."
系列	xì liè	series; set (of related things) — 系 xì (string together) + 列 liè (row; to arrange). A run of related items strung in a row: 系列产品 "a product series," 一系列问题 "a whole series of problems." The connecting 系 makes the separate items read as one ordered set.
系统	xì tǒng	system; systematic — 系 xì (connection) + 统 tǒng (to unify). An ordered whole of interworking parts: 操作系统 "operating system," 神经系统 "nervous system." As an adjective it means systematic: 系统地学习 "to study systematically."
系主任	xì zhǔ rèn	department head
下班	xià bān	to finish work; to get off work; end of shift — 下 xià (to descend; to leave the higher platform) + 班 bān (shift; work roster). To come down from the work platform , to leave one's post at the end of the day. The indispensable pair of 上班. 下班后 (after work) is the default framing for evening activities in Chinese urban conversation.
下蛋	xià dàn	to lay eggs
下课	xià kè	class is over; end of class; to dismiss class — 下 xià (to end; to come down from) + 课 kè (lesson; class). Class has descended from its height, releasing students and teacher. The verb pair 上课/下课 (class begins/class ends) is the daily rhythm of Chinese school life. 下课了！is the joyful announcement that class is over.
下来	xià lái	come down; slow to a stop
下面	xià miàn	below; underneath; the lower surface; the following — 下 xià (below; under) + 面 miàn (face; side; surface). The underside; what is below. Used for physical position (床下面 = under the bed) and for discourse organization (下面请看 = please see the following).
下去	xià qù	continue; go down
下水	xià shuǐ	to enter the water; sewage
下雪	xià xu ě	to snow; snow is falling — 下 xià (to come down; to fall) + 雪 xuě (snow). The standard way to say "it is snowing," built on the same 下 used for 下雨 (to rain). Chinese treats weather that falls from the sky as something the sky "sends down," so snow, rain, and frost all take 下.
下雨	xià yǔ	to rain; it's raining — 下 xià (to descend; to fall from above) + 雨 yǔ (rain). Rain descending from the sky. The 下 construction extends to all precipitation: 下雪 (to snow), 下冰雹 (to hail). Notably, 下 is the standard verb for precipitation in Mandarin , not "fall" or "pour" but "descend." The sky issues weather downward.
下雨了	xià yǔ le	it's raining now; it has started to rain — The canonical example of sentence-final 了 announcing a new situation. There is no past tense here , the rain is starting now, and the 了 signals a change of state has occurred or is occurring. This 了 carries the meaning: "now things are different from before."
下载	xià zài	To download — To download: carry down. Paired with 上传 (upload). 载 also reads zǎi meaning "year" in classical texts, a polyphone trap for advanced readers.
下周	xià zhōu	next week — 下 xià (next; the following) + 周 zhōu (week). The temporal use of 下: what descends next into the present from the future. Consistent across all time units: 下周 (next week), 下个月 (next month), 下年 / 明年 (next year).
吓跑	xià pǎo	to scare away; to frighten off — 吓 (xià, to frighten) + 跑. A V+跑 resultative: the action of frightening produces the result of running off. Highly productive pattern: 赶跑 gǎnpǎo (chase off), 撵跑 niǎnpǎo (drive off), 骂跑 màpǎo (scold off). The construction packages cause and escape into one verb.
夏天	xià tiān	summer
先生	xiān sheng	Mr.; teacher; husband; gentleman — 先 xiān (first; ahead; earlier) + 生 shēng (one born; person). "One born earlier" , originally a teacher or Confucian master; now the standard polite term for "Mr." and also "husband" in formal contexts (我先生 "my husband"). A word whose meaning has drifted beautifully from teacher → gentleman → Mr.
先知	xiān zhī	prophet (Muhammad)
鲜花	xiān huā	fresh flowers; fresh-cut flowers — 鲜 xiān (fresh) + 花 huā (flower). Living, fresh-cut blossoms, the flowers you buy and give: 一束鲜花 "a bouquet of fresh flowers." Distinct from 假花 jiǎhuā, artificial flowers.
鲜美	xiān měi	fresh and delicious; savory — 鲜 xiān (fresh; vivid , the "umami" character, fish + sheep 鱼+羊) + 美 měi (beautiful; delicious). The compound for food that is both fresh and deeply flavorful. Note: 鲜 also contains the sheep radical 羊, linking back to 美's own etymology. Used for broths, seafood, and fresh ingredients.
咸蛋	xián dàn	salted egg
显然	xiǎn rán	obviously; evidently; clearly — 显 xiǎn (visible, to show) + 然 rán (-ly). States that something is plain to see: 显然 he was lying. Often opens a sentence as a comment on the whole: 显然，这是个错误 "clearly, this is a mistake."
险情	xiǎn qíng	dangerous situation
现场	xiàn chǎng	the scene; on-site; live (in person) — 现 xiàn (present) + 场 chǎng (site; field). The very place where something is happening: 案发现场 "the scene of a crime," 现场直播 "a live broadcast."
现成	xiàn chéng	ready-made; off the shelf; already prepared — 现 xiàn (on hand) + 成 chéng (completed). Already finished and available, needing no further making: 现成的答案 "a ready-made answer," 现成的衣服 "off-the-rack clothing."
现实	xiàn shí	reality; actual; (of a person) realistic, practical — 现 xiàn (present) + 实 shí (real; solid). What is actually the case here and now, set against 理想 lǐxiǎng (the ideal). As an adjective for a person it can mean clear-eyed, or pointedly materialistic.
线路	xiàn lù	route, line, circuit
相比	xiāng bǐ	in comparison; compared to
相冲	xiāng chōng	Zodiac conflict — Zodiac conflict: signs that are diametrically opposite in the twelve-year cycle and considered incompatible. The Rat-Horse conflict, the Tiger-Monkey conflict, etc.
相处	xiāng chǔ	to get along; to spend time together — 相 xiāng (each other) + 处 chǔ (to dwell; to be with). To live alongside or get along with others: 容易相处 "easy to get along with," 相处了很多年 "spent many years together."
相反	xiāng fǎn	opposite; contrary
相机	xiàng jī	camera
相克	xiāng kè	mutual conquest; the control cycle — 相 xiāng (mutual) + 克 kè (to conquer, to regulate, to check). In the Five Phases context, 克 means disciplined control rather than destruction , the river bank that contains the flood, not the dam that annihilates the water.
相片	xiàng piàn	photograph; photo — 相 xiàng (appearance) + 片 piàn (a flat slice; sheet). The printed photograph as an object. Its everyday synonym 照片 zhàopiàn is equally common; both name the picture you hold or hang.
相亲	xiāng qīn	to meet a prospective marriage partner; a matchmaking meeting — 相 xiàng (to examine) + 亲 qīn (marriage; relative). The arranged meeting where two people look each other over with marriage in mind, often set up by family or a matchmaker. Note: this 相 keeps a level tone for many speakers, but the sense is the inspecting one.
相生	xiāng shēng	mutual generation; the creative cycle — 相 xiāng (mutual, reciprocal) + 生 shēng (to give birth, to generate, to produce). Each phase nourishes and gives rise to the next; each is simultaneously "mother" to the phase it generates and "child" to the phase that generates it.
相同	xiāng tóng	identical; the same — 相 xiāng (mutually) + 同 tóng (same). Two or more things being the same as each other: 完全相同 "exactly the same." More formal and written than the everyday 一样 yīyàng.
相信	xiāng xìn	to believe; to trust — 相 xiāng (mutual) + 信 xìn (trust). To place trust in someone or to believe a thing is so: 我相信你 "I trust you," 相信科学 "to believe in science." One of the most common verbs built on the mutual reading.
相遇	xiāng yù	to meet; to encounter
湘菜	Xiāng cài	Hunan Cuisine
享有	xiǎng yǒu	to enjoy (a right or privilege)
响应	xiǎng yìng	to respond to; to answer (a call); response — 响 xiǎng (to sound, to echo) + 应 yìng (to respond). To answer a call or appeal, often a collective one: 响应号召 (respond to a call), 积极响应 (respond actively). The image is of an echo answering a sound.
想到	xiǎng dào	to think of; to occur to; to realize — 想 xiǎng (to think; to consider) + 到 dào (arrive). Thinking that arrived at an idea: the thought reached its destination. 想到 implies the idea arrived in mind, often unexpectedly or after searching.
想法	xiǎng fǎ	idea; opinion; what one thinks — 想 xiǎng (to think) + 法 fǎ (method; approach; way). The product of thinking: what thinking produces when it runs long enough. Used freely for ideas, opinions, views, and intentions. 你有什么想法？ is one of the most common ways to ask for someone's opinion in meetings and conversations.
想家	xiǎng jiā	to be homesick; to miss home — 想 xiǎng (to miss) + 家 jiā (home; family; household). The heart facing home from a distance. A verb in Chinese where English uses an adjective ("homesick").
想起	xiǎng qǐ	to recall; to call to mind
想象	xiǎng xiàng	to imagine; imagination — 想 xiǎng (to think) + 象 xiàng (image; likeness; elephant , 象 was borrowed for "image" because of its visual suggestiveness: the elephant is the largest thing you picture). Think-image: to form mental images of things not present. Used as both a verb (to imagine) and a noun (imagination).
想要	xiǎng yào	to want; to desire (explicit) — 想 xiǎng (want; think) + 要 yào (want; need; will). The two wanting words stacked for explicitness or emphasis. Where 想 alone can be tentative (I'd like to; I'm thinking of), 想要 is more direct and pointed. Used when desire is clear and conscious rather than a vague inclination.
向来	xiàng lái	always (up to now); from the start; hitherto — 向 xiàng (facing back along) + 来 lái (coming up to now). A close synonym of 一向, slightly more literary: 他向来诚实 "he has always been honest." Frames a quality as having held from the beginning to the present.
向前	xiàng qián	forward; toward the front; onward — 向 xiàng (toward) + 前 qián (front). Directed at the space ahead: 向前走 "walk forward," 向前看 "look ahead," both literally and in the sense of looking to the future rather than dwelling on the past.
向往	xiàng wǎng	to yearn for; to long for; to look forward to — 向 xiàng (toward) + 往 wǎng (to go toward). The heart facing a place or life it longs for: 向往自由 "to yearn for freedom," 向往美好的生活 "to long for a good life." Warmer and more aspirational than plain "to want."
向阳	xiàng yáng	facing the sun; sunny (of an aspect) — 向 xiàng (toward; facing) + 阳 yáng (sun). Oriented toward the sun, said of a room, window, or slope that gets good light. A 向阳的房间 is a sought-after sunny room.
向右	xiàng yòu	turn right; toward the right — 向 (toward; in the direction of) + 右 (right). The standard directional command for turning right: 向右转 (turn right), 向右走 (go right). As with its mirror pair 向左, used in navigation, military drill, physical education, and any instruction involving directional movement.
向左	xiàng zuǒ	turn left; toward the left — 向 (toward; in the direction of) + 左 (left). The standard instruction for turning or moving left: 向左转 (turn left), 向左走 (go left, walk left). Used in navigation, traffic directions, dance instruction, and any context requiring directional movement.
巷子	xiàng zi	alley, lane
小乘	xiǎo chéng	Theravada — the Lesser Vehicle (pejorative Mahayana term)
小聪明	xiǎo cōng ming	cheap cleverness; cunning without wisdom — Small cleverness: the shortcut-finding wit that games the rule and misses the point. 耍小聪明 (deploying petty cleverness) is what teachers accuse corner-cutting students of; its grown-up antonym is 大智慧, great wisdom.
小弟	xiǎo dì	this humble younger brother (self-deprecating first person) — Used by a male speaker to refer to himself deferentially, regardless of birth order. The formula downsizes the speaker relative to the listener. Also used literally for a younger brother. In gangster/triad contexts, 小弟 means a low-ranking follower , the hierarchy is explicit.
小哥	xiǎo gē	young fellow (polite address)
小狗	xiǎo gǒu	puppy, little dog — 小 (xiǎo, small) + 狗. The everyday word for a puppy or any small dog. 小狗 is warm and affectionate, the term a child reaches for first.
小孩	xiǎo hái	child; kid — 小 xiǎo (small) + 孩 hái (child). The standard neutral word for a child. Related: 小朋友 xiǎopéngyou (young friend , the standard address for children, used by teachers and adults), 小孩子 xiǎoháizi (a slightly more affectionate variant with the diminutive 子).
小河	xiǎo hé	small river; creek
小鸡	xiǎo jī	chick, baby chicken — 小 (xiǎo, small) + 鸡 (chicken). The fluffy yellow chick just out of the shell, a favorite in children's songs and picture books.
小姐	xiǎo jiě	miss; young lady — 小 (small, young) plus 姐 is the classic polite title for a young, unmarried woman, like English "Miss." Register matters: in some mainland contexts a bare 小姐 can sound off, so service settings often prefer 美女 (měinǚ) or a surname plus 姐.
小令	xiǎo lìng	Short lyric form — Short lyric form: tune patterns of 58 characters or fewer. The most concentrated 词 form, requiring the greatest compression. Contrasted with 长调 chángdiào or 慢词 màncí, the extended long forms of 91 characters or more.
小名	xiǎo míng	Childhood nickname , a pet name used within the family, often deliberately humble or silly to avoid attracting bad luck to the child. 狗蛋 (Gǒudàn, "Dog Egg") or 二毛 (Èrmáo, "Second Fuzz") are classic examples.
小女	xiǎo nǚ	my humble daughter (respectful reference) — A father's way of referring to his daughter when speaking to someone of higher status. The 小 does not mean the daughter is young or small , it encodes the father's self-positioning as lesser. Compare 犬子 quǎnzǐ (my unworthy son , lit. "dog son"), the parallel self-deprecating term for sons.
小声	xiǎo shēng	quiet; softly; in a low voice — 小 xiǎo (small) + 声 shēng (voice). A small voice, the opposite of 大声: 小声说 "to speak quietly," often so others do not overhear.
小时	xiǎo shí	hour (unit of time) — 小 xiǎo (small) + 时 shí (time; hour). A small slice of time: one hour. The compound reflects a traditional view of time's granularity , the hour as the smallest standard unit. Contrast 小时候 xiǎoshíhou (childhood, "when one was small"), where 小时 carries the older sense of "young age."
小时候	xiǎo shí hou	in childhood; when (one) was young — "When little," a fixed expression for one's childhood. 我小时候 = "when I was a child." Compare 那时候 (back then, at that time) and 到时候 (when the time comes). Note: 小时 (without 候) means "an hour," a different word, so the 候 matters.
小说	xiǎo shuō	novel; fiction — 小 xiǎo (small; minor) + 说 shuō (speech; theory). Literally "minor talk" , the genre of fiction was long considered a lower form of literature compared to history and philosophy. The term was coined to contrast with 大道 "the Great Way" of classical learning.
小心	xiǎo xīn	be careful; take care; cautious — 小 xiǎo (small) + 心 xīn (heart; mind). A small heart is one drawn in, watchful, attentive to fine detail. The literal image captures the bodily experience of caution: the heart pulling back, contracting, alert to what is delicate or dangerous.
小心眼	xiǎo xīn yǎn	petty-minded; quick to take offense — Small heart-aperture: a mind with a narrow gate, through which slights enter easily and never leave. The grudge-keeping cousin of 小气, aimed at temperament rather than wallet.
小篆	xiǎo zhuàn	small seal script — the Qin standardized writing form — Lesser Seal Script , standardized by the Qin minister Li Si as part of the First Emperor's unification project. Characterized by even-weight strokes, systematic construction, and balanced proportions.
小组	xiǎo zǔ	small group
孝心	xiào xīn	filial intention; filial love
校长	xiào zhǎng	school principal; university president — 校 xiào (school) + 长 zhǎng (elder; head; chief). The head of a school at any level, from primary school principal to university president. The pattern [institution] + 长 = head of that institution is extraordinarily productive: 院长 (dean), 部长 (minister), 市长 (mayor), 省长 (governor), 班长 (class monitor).
笑话	xiào hua	a joke; to laugh at (someone) — 笑 xiào (laugh) + 话 huà (speech; words). Laugh-words: a joke, a funny story. As a verb it flips to "to laugh at, to make a fool of." 讲笑话 = to tell a joke; 闹笑话 = to make an embarrassing blunder that others laugh at.
笑脸	xiào liǎn	smiling face; a smile worn for others — 笑 (laugh, smile) + 脸 (face). A face shaped into a smile, especially the welcoming one you put on for guests or customers. The opposite expression is 板着脸 (bǎnzhe liǎn), to hold the face stiff and stern.
笑着说	xiào zhe shuō	said with a smile; said, smiling — The canonical attendant circumstance: 笑着 (smiling) + 说 (to say). The 着 links the two actions , the smiling frames and accompanies the speaking. This construction is the default way to describe facial expression or gesture accompanying speech in Chinese narrative.
谐音	xié yīn	Near-homophone, sound-alike , the phonetic mechanism underlying most Chinese number symbolism. 谐音 relationships exist wherever two syllables sound similar enough that one can evoke the other.
写法	xiě fǎ	method of writing a character; stroke order
写实	xiě shí	realism; to depict reality faithfully
写下来	xiě xià lái	write it down — For technical terms and every figure. Hand over your notebook or phone. Pinyin input is fine; most people will type the characters for you.
写信	xiě xìn	to write a letter
写意	xiě yì	impressionist; expressive — capturing spirit over likeness
写照	xiě zhào	a portrayal; a true reflection of reality
写真	xiě zhēn	portrait photograph; lifelike depiction — 写 xiě (to write; to depict) + 真. To depict the real. Originally a classical term for a lifelike portrait painting; in modern usage, a posed portrait photo (especially of a model or celebrity). In Japanese, the same word 写真 shashin simply means "photograph."
写字	xiě zì	to write characters; to write by hand — 写 xiě (to write) + 字 zì (character). The fundamental literacy act: hand meeting surface, character taking form. Specifically implies handwriting rather than typing or printing. In Chinese civilization, handwriting is never merely functional.
写作	xiě zuò	to write; to compose — literary register
心得	xīn dé	insight from experience; what one gained
心动	xīn dòng	heart moved; tempted
心房	xīn fáng	atrium of the heart
心即理	xīn jí lǐ	the mind is principle — Wang Yangming's direct challenge to Zhu Xi. Principle 理 is not located in external things requiring investigation; it is not separate from the moral mind. The mind is itself the site of 理.
心经	Xīn jīng	the Heart Sutra · 260 characters · Xuanzang's 649 CE translation — The shortest and most chanted Buddhist scripture in East Asia. The condensed essence of the Prajñāpāramitā literature: emptiness applied to every Buddhist category in turn, then the closing mantra. Memorized by every Chan novice; recited daily across China, Japan, Korea, and Vietnam.
心里	xīn lǐ	in one's heart; in one's mind; emotionally — 心 (heart, mind) + 里 (interior). Functions as a locative: something is in the heart, occurs in the heart. Covers both cognitive interior (I understand in my mind) and emotional interior (I feel in my heart). More intimate and less clinical than 内心 nèixīn (inner heart , a literary register).
心里话	xīn lǐ huà	sincere words; what one truly means — 心里 (inside the heart) + 话 (speech, words). The words that come from the interior rather than from performance. Sharing 心里话 is an act of trust and intimacy , it signals that the speaker is not managing their presentation but revealing what is actually held inside.
心里有数	xīn lǐ yǒu shù	to have a clear grasp; to know what's what — 心里 (inside the heart) + 有 (to have) + 数 (numbers, a count). To hold the numbers privately in one's interior , to have a clear reckoning of a situation that one carries internally.
心理	xīn lǐ	psychology; mental state; mindset — 心 xīn (heart) + 理 lǐ (logic; principle; grain of wood). "The logic of the heart." Used both as the general term for one's psychological state and as the root of 心理学 (psychology as a discipline).
心灵	xīn líng	soul; inner spirit
心情	xīn qíng	mood; state of mind — 心 xīn (heart) + 情 qíng (emotion; situation). The weather of the inner world. Where 情 alone is broad, covering emotion, circumstance, and romantic feeling, 心情 focuses it: the specific emotional atmosphere a person inhabits right now. It fluctuates by the hour.
心学	xīn xu é	School of Mind; Wang Yangming's Neo-Confucian tradition — The Neo-Confucian tradition associated with Wang Yangming. Holds that 理 is not separate from the mind; the moral faculty 良知 is the ground of both knowledge and action.
心眼	xīn yǎn ( r )	wits; smarts; (also) intent, motive of the heart — 心 xīn (heart) + 眼. The eye of the heart , the inner faculty of judgment and quick-wittedness. 心眼多 xīnyǎn duō "to have many wits" (clever, calculating, or scheming, depending on tone). 心眼好 xīnyǎn hǎo "to have a good heart-eye" (kind-hearted, well-intentioned).
心意	xīn yì	intention; goodwill; the feeling behind a gesture — 心 xīn (heart) + 意 yì (intention; meaning). The feeling or thought behind an action: the sincerity and warmth that a gift, gesture, or visit carries. Often used when downplaying the material value of what you have brought: 这只是一点心意 ("this is just a small token of my regard").
心脏	xīn zàng	the heart (anatomical organ) — 心 xīn + 脏 zàng (internal organ, the biological sense; same character used in 器官, organs of the body). In modern Chinese, 心 alone almost never refers to the biological organ; that role belongs entirely to 心脏. The standalone 心 is always the conceptual, emotional, and cognitive heart.
新华字典	Xīn huá Zì diǎn	the Xinhua Dictionary — 新华 Xīnhuá (New China, the name of the official news agency and many state institutions) + 字典. The most widely-sold book in Chinese history and the standard reference for mainland Chinese students since its first edition in 1953. Simple, portable, and comprehensive for modern literacy needs.
新婚	xīn hūn	newly married; newlywed — 新 (new) + 婚 (hūn, marriage). Newly married , in the fresh state of the new relationship. 新婚快乐 (congratulations on the marriage , Happy Newlywed); 新婚夫妇 (newlywed couple); 新郎 xīnláng (groom , new-man) and 新娘 xīnniáng (bride , new-woman) are the standard terms for a wedding couple.
新疆	Xīn jiāng	Xinjiang
新年	xīn nián	New Year — 新 (new) + 年 (year). The new year , the most basic New Year compound, usable for both the Gregorian New Year (January 1) and the Chinese Lunar New Year. 新年快乐 (Happy New Year) and 新年好 (New Year greetings) are the standard phrases.
新年快乐	xīn nián kuài lè	Happy New Year , literally "new year happy — Happy New Year , literally "new year happy." The standard greeting for the solar New Year (January 1). For Lunar New Year, 新春快乐 (xīnchūn kuàilè) or 恭喜发财 (gōngxǐ fācái, "congratulations and may you prosper") are more appropriate.
新儒家	xīn rú jiā	New Confucianism
新闻	xīn wén	news
新鲜	xīn xiān	fresh; novel; new and interesting — 新 (new) + 鲜 (xiān, fresh , especially of food; vivid; rare). Fresh in the sensory sense: 新鲜蔬菜 (fresh vegetables), 新鲜空气 (fresh air). Also used for novel experiences: 对我来说这是个新鲜事物 (this is something new to me). 鲜 alone means fresh-vivid; 新鲜 combines freshness with newness.
信封	xìn fēng	envelope — 信 (xìn, letter) plus 封 (the sealing/wrapper): the paper sleeve that closes around a letter. The most common everyday word built on 封.
信号	xìn hào	signal; indicator — 信 xìn (trustworthy communication; word) + 号 hào (sign; designation; mark; sound used as a signal). A signal is a word that has been stripped of language and reduced to a reliable mark: it carries only its presence or absence, its strength or weakness, rather than semantic content.
信件	xìn jiàn	letters; correspondence (as a category) — 信 xìn (letter; trust) + 件 jiàn (a piece). A collective, somewhat formal term for letters and written correspondence. A single letter is 一封信 (using 封 fēng); 信件 names the whole category of correspondence, the kind of word a post office or office form would use.
信任	xìn rèn	trust; to trust; to have confidence in — 信 xìn (trustworthiness; to trust) + 任 rèn (to appoint; to entrust; to allow , 任 carries the sense of delegating responsibility to someone). To trust is to appoint a person as worthy of reliance. 信任危机 xìnrèn wéijī = a crisis of trust (institutional or personal). 建立信任 jiànlì xìnrèn = to build trust.
信息	xìn xī	information; news; data — 信 xìn (trustworthy word; communication) + 息 xī (breath; rest; news , 息 contains 自 zì, nose, over 心 xīn, heart: breath as the sign of life; by extension, news and tidings). A word that travels and carries meaning , information as a form of 信. 信息时代 xìnxī shídài = the information age.
信用	xìn yòng	creditworthiness; credit; trustworthiness in financial conduct — 信 xìn (trustworthiness) + 用 yòng (use; to use; practical application). Trustworthiness made actionable , the record of a person or institution reliably standing by its commitments. 信用卡 xìnyòng kǎ = credit card. 信用评级 xìnyòng píngjí = credit rating.
星期	xīng qī	week; day of the week — 星 (star) + 期 (period): the star period, from the seven-luminary week. Days attach numbers: 星期三 is Wednesday. Asking the day is 今天星期几? , one of the few questions where 几 needs no measure word.
星宿	xīng xiù	the lunar mansions
星星	xīng xing	Star, in the affectionate reduplicated form used in speech and songs. Single 星 does the compounding work: 星空 (starry sky), 星系 (galaxy, "star system"), 星座 (constellation, and the zodiac sign small talk runs on).
星座	xīng zuò	constellation; zodiac sign — 星 + 座 (seat): the seats of the stars. In young urban small talk it means the Western zodiac sign: 你是什么星座的? has largely displaced asking one's Chinese zodiac animal among the under-forty, an ironic import given the antiquity of native Chinese astral lore.
姓名	xìng míng	full name — surname + given name — 姓 xìng (surname) + 名 míng (given name). The formal term for a full name. Note: Chinese names are surname-first, given-name-second, the reverse of Western convention. 你叫什么名字？= "What is your name?" (informal, just the name). 请问您的姓名？= "May I ask your full name?" (formal).
胸有乾坤	xiōng yǒu qián kūn	"heaven and earth in the breast" — a person of sweeping strategic vision and depth — 胸 xiōng (chest; breast; the seat of mind and intention in classical Chinese thought) + 有 (to have; to hold) + 乾坤. A grand classical expression of praise for someone who holds the entire world in mind: a strategist, a statesman, a general, a writer who sees at the largest possible scale.
修改	xiū gǎi	to revise, to amend, to edit — 修 (mend, trim) + 改 (alter). To go back over a text, plan, or law and revise it. The standard word for editing a document or amending a draft.
羞耻心	xiū chǐ xīn	sense of shame
秀才	xiù cai	the lowest examination degree; a local scholar — The degree produced by the local examination (童试), the entry point of the examination ladder. A 秀才 had cleared the first hurdle and earned a recognized scholarly identity, but stood far from power.
虚实	xū shí	deficient and substantial
虚心	xū xīn	open-minded; receptive; humble in one's willingness to learn — 虚 xū (empty; hollow; receptive , the vessel with space in it) + 心 xīn (heart; the seat of thought and feeling in Chinese understanding). An empty heart , a mind that has not pre-filled itself with conclusions and so can genuinely receive what it encounters.
虚心学习	xū xīn xu é xí	to study with an open mind; to learn receptively — A fixed phrase in educational and developmental discourse. The combination of 虚心 (open-minded) + 学习 (to study; to learn) makes the stance toward learning explicit: approach the subject without assuming you already know, without defending existing positions, with the mind empty enough to receive new…
需要	xū yào	to need; to require (necessity, not merely desire) — 需 xū (to need; to require, a more formal or objective necessity) + 要. 需要 focuses on objective need rather than personal desire. 我需要帮助 (I need help , genuinely require it) vs. 我要帮助 (I want help , requesting it).
许可	xǔ kě	permission (formal noun)
宣读	xuān dú	to read out formally (a statement, verdict, decree) — 宣 xuān (to announce; to proclaim) + 读. To read aloud in a formal setting , a court verdict, a wedding vow, an official document at a ceremony. The 宣 marks the public, declarative force.
悬河	xuán hé	Suspended river , a river whose bed has been raised by sediment deposition above the level of the surrounding plain, contained only by embankments. The lower Yellow River is the world's most extreme example, running up to 10 meters above the adjacent farmland.
选票	xuǎn piào	ballot: a ballot paper; a vote (as a counted unit) — 选 xuǎn (to choose; to elect) + 票 piào (slip; ballot). The physical ballot paper you mark and cast, and by extension a vote as a unit to be counted. The tally of votes is 票数 piàoshù, and to win is to gather the most 选票. Here 票 is the slip that carries a single person's choice.
学坏	xu é huài	to pick up bad habits, to be led astray — 学 (to learn) + 坏 (bad). To learn the wrong things, the classic parental worry that good company makes good children and bad company corrupts them. 坏 here works as a result complement: the learning turns out badly.
学会	xu é huì	to master, to learn (successfully) — Study + acquire-the-skill. 学了三年 states duration; 学会了 states victory. The complement 会 restricts itself to learnable skills, which is a nice grammatical definition of what a skill is.
学了三年	xu é le sān nián	studied for three years (bounded period) — 了 with a duration complement signals an action sustained for a period of time and treated as a completed, bounded episode. This construction is essential for talking about study, work, residence, and sustained experience. The duration frames the action as a finished chunk of time.
学生	xu é sheng	student — 学 xué (to study; to learn) + 生 shēng (one who is still "green"; a junior person). The student as someone still growing, not yet ripe. One of the most common nouns in Chinese , every school context relies on it.
学识	xu é shi	scholarly learning; erudition
学问	xu é wèn	learning; knowledge; scholarship — 学 xué (to study) + 问 wèn (to ask). Knowledge built by studying and asking, the deep, earned kind. 很有学问 "highly learned." The word itself teaches that real knowledge grows from questions, not answers alone.
学习	xu é xí	to study; to learn (ongoing process) — 学 xué (to learn) + 习 xí (to practice; to review; to get accustomed to). The compound captures the Confucian dual process: 学 (receiving new knowledge) + 习 (practicing and consolidating it). Not just acquisition but repeated reinforcement. The most common word for "to study" in general.
学校	xu é xiào	school
学业	xu é yè	studies; schoolwork; academic work — 学 xué (to learn) + 业 yè (course of work). One's studies taken as a whole: 完成学业 "to complete one's studies," 学业成绩 "academic results." The general term for the body of work a student carries.
学院	xu é yuàn	college, institute
学者	xu é zhě	scholar; academic; researcher — 学 (to study) + 者. The standard modern word for a scholar or academic researcher. This compound has been in continuous use since at least the Han dynasty. A 学者 is someone whose work consists of systematic study , the word appears in book reviews, conference programs, and academic citations alike.
雪白	xu ě bái	snow-white; pure, dazzling white — 雪 xuě (snow) + 白 bái (white). The brightest, purest white, with snow as the standard of comparison, exactly parallel to English "snow-white." This is a productive pattern in Chinese: a vivid object plus a color word yields an intensified shade.
雪耻	xu ě chǐ	to wipe out a humiliation
雪花	xu ě huā	snowflake — 雪 xuě (snow) + 花 huā (flower). A snowflake, the snow seen as a falling blossom. The flower metaphor is old and pervasive in Chinese: snow falling is 雪花飘 (snow-flowers drifting), and the six-petaled structure of a real snow crystal was noticed early, with classical texts remarking that snow flowers h…
雪人	xu ěr én	snowman
雪域	xu ě yù	Snow Land — Snow Land: a poetic name for Tibet, used in both Chinese and Tibetan contexts. Refers to the snowcapped mountains and high plateau character of the Tibetan landscape and appears in poetry, song, and political rhetoric from multiple perspectives.
询问	xún wèn	to inquire; to ask about; to seek information — 询 xún (to inquire) + 问 wèn (to ask). A formal verb for asking in order to gather information, common in writing and service contexts: 询问情况 "to inquire about the situation," 向某人询问 "to make inquiries of someone."
荀子	Xún zǐ	Xunzi
训练	xùn liàn	to train; training — 训 (to instruct, to discipline) + 练 (to drill). Organized, directed training: athletes, soldiers, dogs, new staff. Heavier and more institutional than 练习.
压力	yā lì	pressure; stress (physical or mental) — 压 yā (to press down) + 力 lì (force). The force pressing from outside. In physics it is pressure on a surface; in daily life it is the weight of stress: 工作压力 "work stress," 压力很大 "under a lot of pressure."
压岁钱	yā suì qián	lucky money for children at New Year — New Year's money, given in red envelopes; literally "money that suppresses the year-beast." The year-beast (岁 suì) was a mythological monster; the money and noise were protective against it.
鸭蛋	yā dàn	duck egg; a zero (slang) — 鸭 (yā, duck) + 蛋: a duck egg, larger and richer than a hen's. Among students it means a score of zero, since the round 0 on a test paper looks like an egg sitting there.
揠苗助长	yà miáo zhù zhǎng	pulling up seedlings to help them grow
烟火	yān huǒ	fireworks; smoke and fire; the warmth of lived life — 烟 yān (smoke) + 火 huǒ (fire). Literally: fireworks. Poetically: 人间烟火 rénjiān yānhuǒ , "the smoke and fire of the human world" , the warmth and vitality of ordinary life.
严重	yán zhòng	serious — Say plainly how serious it is; understatement is heard as "not urgent." 影响很大 (the impact is large) and 不能接受 (bù néng jiēshòu, cannot be accepted) are firm without being rude.
炎热	yán rè	blazing hot (summer)
研究方向	yán jiū fāng xiàng	research direction; field of specialization — The standard academic term for one's research focus. Appears on every Chinese CV, every graduate-school admission form, and every department web page. 你的研究方向是什么? — "What's your research direction?" — is the second question Chinese academics ask each other after the name.
颜色	yán sè	color (general term) — 颜 yán (color; face , the face radical) + 色 sè. The standard compound for "color." 什么颜色？"What color?" Also used figuratively: 给你点颜色看看 gěi nǐ diǎn yánsè kànkan = "I'll show you what I'm capable of" (lit. "let you see some color" , a threat or challenge to someone who has been underestimating you).
颜色象征	yán sè xiàng zhēng	Color Symbolism — 红 hóng (red) , the color of luck, celebration, life force. Worn at weddings, hung at new year, placed in red envelopes (红包 hóngbāo). 白 bái (white) , the color of mourning; worn at funerals, avoided at celebrations.
颜真卿	Yán Zhēn qīng	Yan Zhenqing — Tang 楷书 master
眼光	yǎng uāng	vision; taste; judgment; the discerning eye — 眼 yǎn + 光 guāng (light). Literally eye-light.
